# 13 Backlog and decisions

## Decision log

Decisions are listed roughly in the order they were made. Each entry says **what was decided**, **why**, and **what the alternative was**.

### Explicit live-mode guard for setup_intent.succeeded (PR #319, pre-live-enablement, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-02):** as the FIRST pre-work step before the live-payment enablement sequence (and deliberately BEFORE relaxing any DB safety rail), close the one card-WRITE webhook path that had no live-mode dormancy guard. **Why:** `setup_intent.succeeded` (`handleSetupIntentSucceeded`, app/api/stripe/webhook/route.ts) writes `client_payment_methods` and, unlike the four money-reconciliation handlers, did not call `shouldIgnoreLiveModeEvent` — so a live-mode event (e.g. a mis-delivered or early live SetupIntent) could have inserted/updated a live card row while live mode is structurally disabled everywhere else. **Change:** `shouldIgnoreLiveModeEvent` is now `export`ed from `lib/billing/payment-webhook-reconciliation.ts` (single source of truth for the guard + its `stripe_webhook_livemode_event_ignored` warning alert), and `handleSetupIntentSucceeded` calls it as its FIRST step (before the metadata/lineage reads and both `client_payment_methods` writes). A live event records the warning ops alert and returns a sanitized `{eventType, setupIntentId, livemodeEventIgnored:true}` summary **without throwing** — so the route marks the event processed (idempotent; no retry storm) and no card row is written and no Stripe API is called. Test-mode events (livemode false) fall through unchanged. **Alternatives considered:** an inline guard duplicated in route.ts (rejected — reusing the exported guard keeps the alert taxonomy + livemode-mismatch semantics identical to the money handlers); relaxing anything for live (explicitly out of scope — this only ADDS dormancy). **Constraints honored:** no migration, no env change, no Stripe write-path change, no live enablement; webhook idempotency + signature verification preserved. Pinned by `tests/lib/billing/payment-webhook-reconciliation-source.test.ts` (guard exported, imported by the route, called before any card write, returns-not-throws, no PII in the summary). `check-stripe-gates` stays 13 PASS; the `live-mode-disabled` safety-lock still passes; production stays at 0100. Next pre-work: PR #320 (charge descriptions by charge_reason + requires_action handling), PR #321 (check-stripe-gates rename-evasion hardening + docs/16 fixes), then the enablement sequence PR #322/#323/#324.

### Supervised pilot readiness checklist + inspection-artifact fixes (PR #318, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-02):** a 12-dimension read-only pilot-readiness audit (booking, intake, client record, charting, photos, Record Keeping, dashboard, print/export, ops alerts, storage safety, payments-disabled, support/rollback) found **0 P0** and a small set of P1s — mostly config/checklist items plus two genuine inspection-artifact correctness bugs. Ship the practical go/no-go checklist + the two code fixes. **Why:** the pilot needs a repeatable "is this studio ready" gate, and the official inspection artifacts must be accurate. **Changes (all display/query/docs, no schema):** (1) New **`docs/21_SUPERVISED_PILOT_CHECKLIST.md`** — go/no-go summary; per-studio must-configure (active + consultation service, weekly availability, correct timezone, slug/name/address, email env, `CRON_SECRET`); pre-pilot verifications (`verify-production.mjs`, treatment-image bucket private + `storage.objects` service-role-only, external reminder-scheduler health); day-1 test plan; intentionally-disabled list (live payments, deposits, returning-client self-booking, SMS-unless-configured); and a **Data recovery** subsection (Supabase backups/PITR, plan-tier check, expected RPO/RTO, who-can-restore, restore-drill recommendation). States plainly that **live payments remain disabled**. (2) **`getClientProcedureRecords`** sessions query gains `.is("deleted_at", null)` (migration 0013 soft-delete) so a session deleted as a correction no longer appears in Procedure Records / the inspection print/export; lot traceability was already block-filtered and is unchanged. (3) The unfiltered procedures **print** shows an honest truncation notice ("Showing the most recent 30 records. Filter by client for a complete procedure log.") when the studio-wide cap is hit — the cap (30) is now the named `UNFILTERED_PROCEDURE_RECORD_LIMIT` (value unchanged). (4) Returning-client booking copy fixed: the portal card no longer promises "book follow-up sessions" (portal can't self-book) → "Sign in to your secure client portal to manage your upcoming appointments." **CRON_SECRET decision:** documented in the checklist only, deliberately NOT added to `check-production-env-gates.mjs` — a required-gate would break the production build with a false failure if the secret isn't already set (`lib/cron/auth.ts` already rejects when unset, and `reminder-heartbeat.ts` alerts if the scheduler goes silent). **Alternatives considered:** raising/removing the 30-cap (rejected — the per-client filter → 200 and the ZIP export are the complete-log paths; a browse cap + honest notice is right); building returning-client online booking (rejected — out of scope; supervised quick-book is the workaround). **Constraints honored:** no migration/RLS/schema change; no payment/live-mode/env/cron-behavior change; no intake/photo/charting change. Pinned by `tests/app/records/procedure-record-filter.test.ts` (soft-delete filter + cap notice + named constant), `tests/app/pilot-readiness-318.test.ts` (booking copy + checklist content + no-DDL), and `tests/db/procedure-records-soft-delete.db.test.ts` (real-DB soft-delete exclusion). Stripe gates remain 13 PASS; production stays at 0100.

### Record Keeping pilot QA polish — print expiry marker + "expires today" (PR #317, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-02):** a read-only pilot QA pass over Record Keeping after PR #316 found **0 P0/P1 issues** (the feature is polished, studio-scoped, tested, and consistent with the disinfectant due-status pattern). Ship the small polish items it surfaced. **Why:** the one genuine rough edge was that the **print/export inspection artifact didn't flag expiry** — `SterilePrint` showed the raw expiry date but the red/amber cue lived only in the interactive list (color doesn't print), so a printed/PDF'd record didn't make an expired lot obvious. **Changes (all display/query-test, no schema):** (1) `SterilePrint` appends a **plain-text, print-safe marker** to the Expiry date — "(expired)" / "(expires today)" / "(expires soon)" — via the existing `supplyExpiryPrintMarker` + `todayInTz(studio.timezone)`, only when an expiry is recorded. (2) A short **export README** note that expiry status is derivable from `expiry_date` (CSV schema unchanged). (3) **"expires today" parity** with disinfectant `due_today`: the pure `supplyExpiryState` gains a `"today"` state + a shared `supplyExpiryLabel` ("Expired" / "Expires today" / "Expires soon") used by the Records badges, dashboard card, and (lowercased) the print marker — one source of truth; `"today"` folds into the banner's "expiring within 30 days" count. (4) A **db-integration test** for `getExpiringSterileItems` exercising the real migrated DB, RLS-scoped: returns expired + today + within-horizon (incl. the today+30 boundary), excludes null-expiry / future-beyond-horizon, and never leaks another studio's row. **Alternatives considered:** color/emphasis in print (rejected — won't print + editorializes the inspection log; plain text is honest); renaming existing "Expired"/"Expires soon" badges to match disinfectant's "— replace now" (rejected — churn/test-breakage, "do not overbuild"). **Constraints honored:** no migration/RLS/schema change; copy-last still never copies `lot_number`; manufacturer custom values still round-trip; no payment/live change; no service-role broadening. Pinned by the extended `tests/lib/record-keeping/supply-expiry.test.ts` + `tests/app/records/probe-expiry-copy-last.test.ts` + the new `tests/db/expiring-sterile-items.db.test.ts`. Stripe gates remain 13 PASS; production stays at 0100.

### Probe/sterile expiry reminders + copy-last + manufacturer dropdown (Chloe feedback, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-02):** ship Chloe's Record Keeping usability asks as a **UI-layer** feature over the existing `record_keeping_sterile_items` table — **no migration**. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback):** remind when probes expire; let her copy the last probe/sterile entry (but never the lot number); make manufacturer a dropdown (Protec/Ballet/Sterex); make expired entries visually obvious in Records. **Finding (inventory):** the table already has `expiry_date` (nullable `date`), `manufacturer_name` (free text, no CHECK), and `lot_number`; RLS is `is_studio_member` (studio-scoped); the Records page already loads the full studio sterile list newest-first (`getSterileItemRecords`), and `getLatestProbeLotSuggestion` already uses `expiry_date >= today` — so the whole feature is presentation + one studio-scoped read, no schema/RLS change. **Implementation:** (1) a **`ManufacturerPicker`** (`record-forms.tsx`) — a controlled `<select>` of Protec/Ballet/Sterex + "Other (type a name)" submitting exactly one `manufacturer_name` via a hidden input; the column stays free text, so existing/legacy values round-trip (edit preselects "Other" + prefills a non-listed value). (2) **Copy-last** — a client-side "Copy last entry" button on the Add form prefills date_purchased/item_description/manufacturer/amount/expiry/notes from the latest already-loaded record and **never `lot_number`** (the `SterileCopyLast` type has no lot field; the Lot # input is never prefilled), with a remount + `onAfterSuccess` clear so a subsequent add starts blank. (3) A pure **`lib/record-keeping/expiry.ts`** (`supplyExpiryState`/`summarizeSupplyExpiry`/`supplyExpiryHorizon`, `SUPPLY_EXPIRING_WITHIN_DAYS = 30`, `today` passed in) drives Records row styling (expired = red + "Expired" badge; ≤30 days = amber + "Expires soon") + a summary banner (N expired · M expiring), studio-local via `todayInTz`. (4) A studio-scoped **`getExpiringSterileItems`** query + a dashboard **"Supplies expiring"** card (`supplies-expiring.tsx`) rendered beside the Follow-up assistant — **no email/SMS/push, no cron**; renders nothing when clear. **Alternatives considered:** forcing the supply alert into the client-centric `MissingRecordItem`/Follow-up assistant (rejected — it's studio-level, not per-client; a separate card is honest); a datalist combobox for manufacturer (rejected — the select+Other is more explicit and preserves legacy values on edit); a migration to constrain manufacturer to an enum (rejected — would break existing free-text values + is unnecessary). **Constraints honored:** no migration/schema/RLS change; copy-last never copies the lot number; studio isolation unchanged (all reads studio-scoped, RLS-backed); no payment/live-mode change. Pinned by `tests/lib/record-keeping/supply-expiry.test.ts` + `tests/app/records/probe-expiry-copy-last.test.ts`. Production stays at 0100; Stripe gates unchanged.

### Storage.objects isolation audit + manual-check reminder (pre-live review, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-02):** after a fresh evidence-based audit of all Supabase Storage usage / `storage.objects` policies (before real treatment photos / client files are trusted at scale), ship only a **docs/test/tooling** hardening — add the missing **storage.objects** manual-check to the `verify-production.mjs` printed reminder and make the docs precise. **Why:** the audit (8 agents, 36 files/migrations, 6 dimensions, adversarial verification) found **0 P0/P1 blockers** — the storage isolation is sound and actually *stronger* than the design summary: single `treatment-images` bucket is private (`public=false`, 0092/0093, verify-production-asserted, no `getPublicUrl` anywhere), 0093 **drops** the 0092 authenticated `storage.objects` select/insert policies and adds none, so `authenticated`/`anon` are **deny-by-default → objects are service-role-only** (pinned behaviorally in the db-integration lane, `treatment-image-hardening.db.test.ts`); the one object write uses a server-derived `<studio_id>/<client_id>/<id>.<ext>` key; every `createSignedUrl` is gated by a studio+client ownership check + `validateTreatmentImagePath`; delete is soft-only + studio-scoped; the studio export excludes binaries/storage paths (PR #312). **The one real (low-severity) observation:** `storage.objects` policies are owned by `supabase_storage_admin` and are **not introspectable** from the linked query role, so their prod state is a manual dashboard check — which docs/16 §17.13 documented but the `verify-production.mjs` *printed* manual-checks section omitted (it listed Vercel env / Stripe dashboard / log sample only). **Fix:** add a precise storage.objects line to the verifier's printed "MANUAL checks still required" section (**print-only** `console.log` string — no logic/behavior change) and sharpen docs/16 §17.13 (service-role-only end state; no `authenticated`/`anon` access to `treatment-images`; no foreign-bucket policy OR-combining onto `storage.objects`). **Alternatives considered:** a migration to re-add explicit deny policies (rejected — deny-by-default is already the RLS default once the authenticated policies are dropped; adding policies would be churn + risk); docs-only with no verifier change (rejected — the operator running the pre-live tool wouldn't be reminded of the one non-introspectable guarantee). **No migration, no RLS/storage-policy change, no service-role broadening, no runtime upload/read/sign/delete change, no payment/live change.** Pinned by `tests/scripts/verify-production.test.ts` (printed manual output names the storage.objects check). Production stays at 0100; live payments remain disabled.

### Service-role inventory + allowlist (pre-live P1, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** pin **every** `createAdminClient()` (service-role) call site in a test-enforced allowlist so an overlooked or new RLS-bypassing usage can't sneak in and break studio isolation. **Why:** the final pre-live review found a **P1** multi-tenant risk — service-role bypasses RLS, most inspected paths look scoped, but future/overlooked usage could accidentally cross studios. **Finding (inventory):** `createAdminClient()` (`lib/supabase/admin-server.ts`) is the **only** service-role constructor (no direct `SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` client elsewhere; other matches are comments); **61 runtime files** call it. The existing `admin-server-boundary.test.ts` protects the *browser* boundary (server-only + no client import) but does **not** inventory server-side call sites. Spot-checks of the highest-risk session-less paths (public token routes, Stripe/Twilio webhooks, cron) confirmed each is token/signature/secret-scoped — **no unsafe usage found, so no runtime change**. **Design (Option 2, strengthened — approved):** a data file `tests/security/service-role-allowlist.ts` (61 entries: `{ path, purpose, why, scopeGuard }`) + an enforcement test `tests/security/service-role-allowlist.test.ts` that (1) greps the live `createAdminClient()` call-site set and asserts it **exactly equals** the allowlist paths (new → fail; removed-but-listed → fail), (2) requires non-empty purpose/why/scopeGuard on every entry, and (3) asserts each **scopeGuard symbol is present in its file** (a dropped guard fails) — turning the allowlist from documentation into an enforced guard. `scopeGuard` names a real symbol: `getCurrentPractitionerWithStudio` (18 authed practitioner actions), `.eq("studio_id"` (15 studio-scoped libs), `isAdmin` (7 admin-console), `verifyCancellationToken`/`verifyIntakeToken`/`hashToken`/`token_hash` (public token routes), `constructEvent` (Stripe webhook), `x-twilio-signature` (Twilio), `isAuthorizedCronRequest`/`CRON_SECRET` (cron), plus `signInWithOtp` (pre-auth login), `studio_id: input.studioId` (notifications), `redactSafeDetails` (ops alerts). A rare `justificationOnly` escape hatch (currently **0** uses; test caps it <5%) requires strong prose where no single symbol captures the guard. **Explicitly an inventory/drift gate, not a per-query proof** — the docs (03 §1a) say deeper audits are still required for high-risk session-less surfaces. **Alternatives considered:** source-grep-only allowlist (rejected — no justification/guard, weak); inline-comment-with-nearby-justification test (rejected — brittle comment-format enforcement); a broad admin→RLS refactor (rejected/out-of-scope — no runtime change unless an unsafe site is found; none was). **No migration, no RLS/schema/storage change, no runtime behavior change, no payment/Stripe/live change.** Pinned by the two new files in `tests/security/`; complements `admin-server-boundary.test.ts`. Stripe gates unchanged (money 1/1/0/0 + non-money pinned + catch-all); production stays at 0100. Docs: 03 §1a (Service-role inventory).

### Record Keeping ZIP/export completeness (pre-live P1, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** add the four record-keeping / inspection tables to the owner-only studio ZIP export — `record_keeping_sterile_items`, `record_keeping_disinfectants`, `record_keeping_exposure_incidents`, and a **reduced** `record_keeping_audit_events` — for inspection, continuity, and paid-customer readiness. **Why:** the final pre-live review found a **P1** — the in-app Record Keeping/print surfaces are useful, but the full studio ZIP export **omitted** the inspection-relevant record-keeping tables, so a departing/inspected studio couldn't get a complete export. **Finding (inspection):** `exportStudioDataAction` is already owner-only (`role === "owner"`), uses the **RLS `createClient()`** (not admin), scopes every query `.eq("studio_id", studio.id)`, writes a README, and logs a fail-closed `audit_logs` `studio_export` entry — a clean insertion point. Migration **0088** makes `record_keeping_exposure_incidents` SELECT **owner-only** and hides exposure-incident audit rows from non-owners; `csvCell` already JSON-stringifies/escapes. **Design (tight, approved):** add four studio-scoped RLS reads + four explicit-header CSVs, preserving the owner gate + audit entry, and a README **SENSITIVE DATA** warning (the ZIP now contains an exposure-incident log with personal info). Exposure incidents are enforced owner-only **twice** — the action's role gate AND the owner-only RLS SELECT (no admin client). **Audit CSV is REDUCED (approved):** `record_type` / `record_id` / `action` / `changed_fields` (names) / `actor_practitioner_id` / `actor_display_name` / `created_at` — the `changes` value-snapshot JSON and free-form `metadata` are deliberately **not** exported, so exposure-incident PII isn't duplicated into a second file. **Deferred (approved):** `treatment_images.csv` metadata (later PR); payment tables; intake export changes. **Explicitly excluded:** image binaries, storage paths/buckets, signed URLs, PDF inspection packet, print-UI changes, schema/RLS/permission changes. **Alternatives considered:** using the admin/service-role client (rejected — would bypass the exposure-incident owner-only RLS; the RLS client is the second safety layer); exporting the full audit `changes`/`metadata` (rejected per the reduced-export decision to avoid PII duplication); including image metadata now (deferred to keep this PR tight). **No migration, no schema/RLS change, no production writes.** Pinned by `tests/app/settings/data/export-owner-gate.test.ts` (four CSVs; studio-scoped RLS reads; owner gate before reads; reduced audit — no `changes`/`metadata`; no image/storage/payment surface; README warning; audit entry preserved) + the `tests/app/records/audit-trail.test.ts` guard (export only READS the audit table, never writes). Stripe gates unchanged (money 1/1/0/0 + non-money pinned + catch-all); production stays at 0100.

### Postcare send-state correctness (pre-live P1, migration 0100)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** stop overclaiming postcare as "sent" when the provider send fails — separate the atomic first-send **claim** from the **sent** marker so `postcare_email_sent_at` is stamped **only after Resend confirms success**, and record an honest **failed** state. **Why:** the final pre-live review found a **P1** — `sendPostcareEmailAction` used `postcare_email_sent_at` as both the `sent_at IS NULL` first-send claim guard **and** the "sent" indicator, stamping it **before** the Resend call and never rolling it back on failure. A failed provider send therefore left `sent_at` set, and the appointment-detail UI (PR #302) showed "Postcare sent {date}" — a delivery overclaim. **Finding (inspection):** the reminder cron already solves the identical problem with a claim/sent/attempts/failed column set (`claim_email_send`, 5-min stale window). **Migration 0100 (approved, full 4-column):** add nullable `postcare_email_claimed_at` / `_failed_at` / `_last_error` / `_last_attempt_at` to `appointments` — additive/backfill-safe, **no RLS** (the action uses the service-role admin client), **no enum/trigger/destructive** change; existing historical `sent_at` rows are left untouched (a legacy send stays "sent"; we can't distinguish a real historical send from a past overclaim, so fix-forward only). **Corrected action:** first send **claims `claimed_at`** (guarded by `sent_at IS NULL` + `(claimed_at IS NULL OR claimed_at < now-5min)`, `.select("id")` row-affected) + sets `last_attempt_at` + increments attempts, **without** stamping `sent_at`; on provider success stamp `sent_at` + clear `_failed_at`/`_last_error`/`_claimed_at`; on failure set `_failed_at` + a **safe/generic** `_last_error` (keyed off `result.retryable`; never the raw Resend payload, client email/name, health data, or exception details) + clear the claim, leaving `sent_at` NULL (a failed **resend** keeps any prior real `sent_at`). A success-write DB error under-claims ("still sending" → stale → "not sent") rather than overclaims. **UI (PostcareSendButton, server-computed `sending` flag so the client render carries no `Date.now` → no hydration mismatch):** Sent / **Send failed — try again** / Sending… / Not-sent, plus resend-failed-after-success = keep "Postcare sent {date}" + a small "Last resend failed. Try again." sub-note. **"Sent" means handed to the provider — never delivered/received/opened/read.** **Alternatives considered:** a no-migration rollback of `sent_at`→NULL on failure (rejected — loses failure visibility, can't show "failed/try-again", and the rollback UPDATE can itself fail); a 3-column migration without `last_attempt_at` (rejected — the user approved the full 4-column version for honest resend timing); showing "delivered/received" (rejected — Hone has no delivery receipts). **No payments/Stripe/live-payment/cron/intake/photo/Record-Keeping/calendar change; no real emails in tests.** Production migration 0099 → **0100**. Pinned by `tests/migrations/0100-postcare-send-state.test.ts` (4 nullable columns; no RLS/enum/trigger/destructive) + `tests/app/calendar/postcare-send-state.test.ts` (claim-via-claimed_at-before-provider; sent-only-on-success; failure sets failed_at + safe last_error + leaves sent_at NULL; attempts/last_attempt_at increment; stale-claim guard; 5 UI states; no delivery overclaim; no raw payload/PII). Stripe gates unchanged (money 1/1/0/0 + non-money pinned + catch-all).

### Payment failure-persistence alert hardening (pre-live P1, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** make **failed/non-success** Stripe-outcome persistence failures as observable as **success**-persistence failures — add the missing **critical** ops alert on the DB-error branch of `writeFailedOutcome` and **promote its zero-row alert from warning to critical**. **Why:** the final pre-live review found a **P1** asymmetry — `writeSucceededOutcome` (PR #281/#263) raises a critical ops alert on both persist-failure modes (real money at stake), but `writeFailedOutcome` logged the DB-error to **stderr only (no ops alert)** and raised only a **warning** on zero rows, so a failed outcome Hone couldn't persist was far less visible (and, being non-critical, absent from the critical-only manual-review queue). **Finding (inspection):** both writers share the same status-conditional `.update().eq('status','pending_stripe').select('id')` shape; the success writer's alert carries `studioId`/`clientId`/`stripePaymentIntentId` + safe `safeDetails`; the manual-review queue filters `severity='critical'` + `PAYMENT_MANUAL_REVIEW_EVENT_PREFIXES` (which includes `session_payment_`); `ops_alerts.event`/`severity` are free-text. **Design (alerting-only, symmetric):** DB-error branch keeps `logInternal` + adds `recordOpsAlert({severity:'critical', event:'session_payment_failed_write_failed', …})`; zero-row branch keeps its event name but is now `critical`; `studioId`/`clientId` are threaded into `writeFailedOutcome`'s args and passed at all **3** call sites (`args.*` in `reconcileExistingPaymentIntent`; `attemptRow.*` in `runSessionPaymentCharge`). `safeDetails` = `attempt_id` / `attempted_status:'failed'` / `stripe_status` / `db_code` — no card details, client names/emails/phones, health/treatment notes, or raw Stripe payload. **The two new critical events surface in `/admin/payments/manual-review` automatically** via the existing `session_payment_` prefix — **no manual-review page change**. No money is at stake on this path (Stripe did NOT succeed), but a stuck `pending_stripe` row diverging from the reported `failed` outcome is now paged like its success counterpart. **Unchanged:** `writeSucceededOutcome`, all Stripe calls (still one `paymentIntents.create`, one `retrieve`), payment-flow return values (the caller still reports `failed`; `return;` on DB error kept), webhook reconciliation, refund logic, the manual-review page, live-mode gates, env, schema/RLS. **Alternatives considered:** leave the failure zero-row at warning (rejected — the review + manual-review-queue visibility want symmetry); change the caller to return `needs_manual_review` on a failure-persist DB error (rejected — no money captured, reporting `failed` is correct; alerting-only is the minimal, safe fix); a new migration/enum for the event (rejected — `ops_alerts.event` is free-text). **No migration; no new Stripe calls; no live-payment enablement; no payment behavior change.** Pinned by `tests/lib/billing/payment-failure-persistence.test.ts` (critical DB-error + zero-row alerts; safe-details-only/no-PII; `studioId`/`clientId` in all 3 call sites; success alerts unchanged; Stripe call counts unchanged; manual-review prefix + stuck-pending unchanged). Stripe gates remain money 1/1/0/0 + non-money pinned + catch-all; production stays at 0099.

### Expand Stripe source gates to all Stripe writes (pre-live review finding, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** turn `scripts/check-stripe-gates.mjs` from a money-movement-only gate into a **complete Stripe-write inventory** — keep money movement byte-identically **1/1/0/0**, add **exactly-count-pinned** rules for the six non-money writes, and add a **hard-fail catch-all** for any unclassified Stripe mutating call. **Why:** the final pre-live review found the gate strong but **too narrow** — it proved money-movement call counts but not *all* Stripe mutating calls, so a new `stripe.*.create/update/cancel/confirm/…` could land unnoticed. **Finding (inventory, catch-all-validated):** runtime source has exactly 8 Stripe writes — 2 money (`paymentIntents.create`, `refunds.create`) + 6 non-money (`customers.create`, `setupIntents.create` in `lib/stripe/setup-intent.ts`; `accounts.create`, `accountLinks.create`, `accounts.createLoginLink` in `lib/stripe/account.ts`; browser `confirmSetup` in `app/portal/PortalPaymentMethodForm.tsx`) — plus 4 read-only `.retrieve` calls; every write already routes through the shared `getStripe()`/`assertStripeKeyAllowed()` + `inferStripeLivemode()` gate (test-mode). **Design (strict, approved):** non-money rules are `exactly: 1` + single-file allowlist (a new site is a deliberate review event); the catch-all matches a namespace-agnostic `stripe.<…>.<write-verb>` shape + browser `confirmSetup/confirmPayment/confirmCardPayment`, subtracts classified occurrences, and hard-fails on any residual, naming the file/method. **Two engine refinements:** (1) the non-money rules opt into **comment-stripping** (`stripComments: true`) so doc-comment mentions of a method don't inflate the exact count — the money rules keep scanning raw source, unchanged; (2) the catch-all strips comments too, so prose never false-fails. **Money output is byte-compatible** with the pre-#309 gate (protects `scripts/verify-production.mjs` + the existing tests). **Alternatives considered:** a new separate script (rejected — would fork the walker/engine and fragment the gate; the existing rule engine extends cleanly); allowlist-only without a count for non-money writes (rejected — the user chose strict exactly-count pinning); warning-first rollout for unknown writes (rejected — hard-fail now). **Source-gate/read-only only:** no runtime/live-mode/payment/refund/SetupIntent/Connect/webhook/env/migration change; no Stripe calls; **live payments remain disabled**. Pinned by `tests/scripts/check-stripe-gates.test.ts` (money 1/1/0/0 exact lines; six pinned non-money rules; catch-all hard-fails on an injected server new-verb, a new namespace, and a browser `confirmPayment`; comment-only + read-only + classified-only do not fail). Stripe gates remain 1/1/0/0; production stays at 0099.

### Read-only production verification script/runbook (P0 pre-live gap, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** add an **operator-run, read-only** `scripts/verify-production.mjs` + a docs/16 §17.13 runbook that proves remote production matches the repo's required state (migration max 0099 + the 0093/0097/0098/0099 effects + private bucket + RLS + zero critical payment ops alerts + Stripe gates + fresh heartbeat) **before** live payments or broader sensitive-data use. **Why:** the final pre-live review found a **P0** — the repo requires prod migration max 0099 but the review couldn't *independently confirm remote prod state*. **Finding (inspection):** the linked Supabase CLI already lets an operator run arbitrary **read-only** SQL (`supabase db query --linked`) — the exact tool used to verify every migration this session — so remote verification needs no new infra; the heartbeat is an Upstash key (`reminder_cron:last_success`, 45-min staleness); the Stripe gates already have `check-stripe-gates.mjs`. **Design:** a `.mjs` script (mirrors the existing `check-*.mjs`) that shells `supabase db query --linked` for scalar-only reads (counts/booleans/version/names), spawns `check-stripe-gates.mjs`, and reads the Upstash heartbeat; it is **read-only** (never `db push`/`db execute`, no migration, no write, no cron, no email, no Stripe write), **prints no secrets/PII/rows**, and **fails closed** (any FAIL/INCOMPLETE → exit non-zero; missing Upstash → heartbeat INCOMPLETE, not PASS). It clearly separates `PRODUCTION VERIFIED ✓` (automated) from the **manual** dashboard checks (Vercel prod env presence, Stripe dashboard, storage.objects policies, optional log sample). **Validated end-to-end read-only against prod during implementation:** 11/11 automated checks PASS, heartbeat INCOMPLETE locally (no Upstash in `.env.local`) → exit 1, exactly as designed. **Alternatives considered:** a CI gate (rejected — CI has no prod link, and requiring prod secrets in CI is unsafe; kept it operator-only); a direct PG connection with the service-role key (rejected — supabase-js can't run the information_schema/pg_* introspection; the linked CLI's `db query` is cleaner and matches the documented process); checking storage.objects policies via `pg_policies` (rejected — not introspectable from the linked query role, returns empty; verify the private-bucket state + `treatment_images` table policies + integrity trigger instead, and defer the storage.objects policy list to the manual runbook). **No migration; no production writes; not a live-payment enablement script.** Pinned by `tests/scripts/verify-production.test.ts` (read-only contract, no Stripe write / no email / no cron, scalar-only/no-PII output, fail-closed, required-check coverage, docs present). Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0); production stays at 0099. Deferred: automating the manual Vercel/Stripe/log checks; a scheduled/alerting version.

### Treatment photo notes/captions (Chloe pilot feedback, migration 0099)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** let practitioners add/edit a short note under each treatment photo, as a plain nullable text column + a metadata-only update — **without touching upload/security/storage**. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback, deferred from PR #304):** she wants a note that belongs to a specific photo. **Finding (inspection):** `treatment_images` (0092) had no note column; the **0093 integrity trigger `enforce_treatment_image_integrity` freezes only the identity columns** (studio/client/bucket/path/session/block), so a new non-identity column can be UPDATEd freely; and the existing **RLS `"treatment_images: members update"` policy already permits studio members to UPDATE the row** (the archive soft-delete uses it) — so **no RLS or trigger change is needed**. **Ponytail pass: additive column + one small action + card/modal editor.** **Migration 0099 (approved):** `add column if not exists practitioner_note text` — nullable/backfill-safe, no RLS/enum/trigger/destructive/storage change. **Action** `updateTreatmentImageNoteAction({imageId, clientId, note})` — a **metadata-only UPDATE via the RLS client** (`createClient()`, not service-role), scoped by `id + studio_id + client_id + deleted_at IS NULL` with a `.select("id")` **row-affected check**, mirroring `archiveTreatmentImageAction` (PR #287) so a same-studio cross-client write is a generic "not found"; trims, whitespace-only → NULL, caps at **1,000 chars** (`TREATMENT_NOTE_MAX_LENGTH`, in a plain `note-constants.ts` module since a `"use server"` file can only export async functions), generic errors, no token/PII log, `revalidatePath`. **UI** (`TreatmentImagesManager`): a `PhotoNoteEditor` under each photo on the **card and modal** — shows the note when present + "Edit note", else "Add note"; inline textarea + Save/Cancel; practitioner-only via the gated page. `page.tsx` selects `practitioner_note` (read-only) → `row.note`; `TreatmentImage` type += `practitioner_note`. **Alternatives considered:** a separate photo_notes table (rejected — a per-photo single caption is a column, not a table); auditing note changes (deferred — practitioner metadata, `updated_at` reflects it; a comms/audit trail is a separate decision); rich text (rejected — plain text, React-escaped). Upload form, sanitizer, storage paths, signed URLs, Archive/delete, and session/block attach are all untouched. Pinned by `tests/migrations/0099-treatment-image-notes.test.ts` (additive; no RLS/trigger/enum/destructive/storage) + `tests/app/clients/treatment-photo-notes.test.ts` (RLS client; id+studio+client+deleted-null scope + row-affected check; trim/null/cap/generic errors; card+modal editor; scope guard). No payment/Stripe/cron/intake/Record-Keeping/calendar change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). Production migration 0098 → **0099**. Deferred: note audit/history.

### Intake form reminder emails (Chloe pilot feedback, migration 0098)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** send automated intake-form reminder emails ~7 days and ~3 days before a confirmed appointment when the client's latest intake is still `in_progress`, **reusing the existing appointment-reminder cron + idempotency model** rather than building a new framework. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback):** clients need nudges to complete intake before their visit, but without spam/duplicates. **Finding (inspection):** the appointment-reminders cron (`/api/cron/appointment-reminders`) uses **column-based per-appointment idempotency** (`<kind>_sent_at`/`_send_attempts`/`_claimed_at`) with an **atomic claim RPC** (`claim_email_send`/`record_email_result`, 0080), a due-window loader, `sendEmailSafely`, `recordOpsAlert`, and a heartbeat — all reusable; and PR #303's `generateIntakeLinkUrl` (pure) + `stampIntakeLinkIssued(admin,…)` + the intake status model are directly reusable by a session-less cron. **Ponytail pass: extend the existing cron; no new subsystem.** **Migration 0098 (approved):** additive/backfill-safe columns on `appointments` for the `7d`/`3d` kinds (`sent_at` nullable timestamptz, `send_attempts` int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, `claimed_at` nullable), two partial due-window indexes (mirroring 0025), and **`create or replace` of `claim_email_send` + `record_email_result`** with the existing confirmation/reminder_24h/reminder_2h branches reproduced **byte-for-byte** plus `intake_reminder_7d`/`intake_reminder_3d` branches — so existing reminder behavior is unchanged. No RLS/enum/destructive change; service_role-only grants re-asserted. **Cron:** a new `IntakeReminderKind` (`7d`/`3d`) + `intakeReminderWindowIso` with **2-hour windows centered on the target day** (≥ the 15-min cadence, so the reliability invariant holds and each appointment gets exactly one reminder per kind); `sendIntakeReminderPass` loads the window, resolves the client's **latest intake with the admin client, studio+client scoped** (isolation preserved), **skips** submitted/reviewed/missing intakes + no-email clients **before** claiming (no wasted attempt), claims-before-send, re-checks confirmed, **always mints a fresh 14-day link** (signed token authoritative; saved answers preserved — reuses the existing intake row), sends `buildIntakeReminderEmail`, records via `record_email_result`, and stamps the PR #303 metadata (`emailed:true`) only on a successful send. Logs only appointment/studio ids + kind — **no raw token, no client PII**; failures reuse `logEmailFailure` + the `reminder_send_exhausted` alert at MAX_ATTEMPTS. **Email copy** is reminder-specific (not the practitioner "please update" copy): subject "Reminder: please complete your intake form before your appointment" (no PII/date), body with appointment date/time + "helps your practitioner prepare safely" + fresh link + "ignore if already completed"; no medical claims, no delivery overclaim. **No per-studio toggle** (the dedupe + skip-submitted + 7d/3d cadence already prevent spam; a `send_intake_reminders` toggle is an easy follow-up). **Alternatives considered:** a new reminder-events table (rejected — diverges from the established column pattern); a NEW dedicated intake claim RPC (rejected — extending the existing RPC keeps one claim path and reuses `claimEmailSend`/`recordEmailResult` unchanged; the added branches are independent so existing reminders are byte-identical); mint-only-when-expired via `computeIntakeLinkStatus` (rejected — always-fresh guarantees the reminder link works and is simpler). Pinned by `tests/migrations/0098-intake-reminder-columns.test.ts` (columns/indexes/RPC branches, existing branches intact, no RLS/enum/destructive), `tests/app/api/cron/intake-reminder.test.ts` (windows; claim-before-send; skip submitted/no-email; fresh link + stamp; safe logging; GET wiring), `tests/lib/email/intake-reminder-template.test.ts` (copy). No payment/Stripe/photo/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). Production migration 0097 → **0098**. Deferred: SMS reminders, per-studio toggle, unlimited cadence, preference center.

### Calendar appointment card visual refresh (Fresha readability inspiration, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** make calendar appointment blocks read as stronger, clearer, scannable blocks with a visible time range and a clear hierarchy — **Fresha used only as a readability benchmark, never copied** (no branding/assets/icons/exact palette/layout). **Why (Chloe feedback on a Fresha reference):** Hone's cards were ultra-pale (`bg-*-50`), showed only a start time + duration (no range), and mushed service/modality inline with the time at low emphasis — the day shape wasn't immediately readable. **Ponytail pass: presentational (CSS/JSX) only — no booking logic, no smart-scheduling, no data/query change, no positioning math, no migration.** Changes (smallest refresh, the highest-impact card piece only): (1) `lib/calendar/service-colors.ts` — deepen the per-service palette from `bg-*-50`/`border-l-*-400` to **`bg-*-100`/`border-l-*-500`** (calm, clinical — not salon-bright); the deterministic djb2 hash + UUID-stability + neutral fallback are unchanged, and the **rose/red family stays excluded** (Hone reserves it for allergy/EpiPen/clinical-caution). (2) `DayColumn.tsx` — the appointment card left accent `border-l-[3px]` → **`border-l-4`**; a visible **time range** ("9:00–10:00") derived from the existing `starts_at`/`ends_at`; and a **3-line hierarchy** (time range → bold client name → service/modality on its own line) for tall cards, with the short-block fallback kept to one dense line. (3) `calendar-format.ts` — a small pure `timeRangeLabel(start24, end24)` (strips the leading zero, en-dash, start-only fallback). Terminal/cancelled opacity, the card click/link (`/calendar/[id]` + return context), and the grid positioning math are all unchanged. Blocked-time cards keep their own `border-l-[3px]` tan treatment (untouched). **Alternatives considered:** going to `-200`/`-600` (rejected — too heavy, breaks the calm/clinical requirement); folding in the open-hours band + gridline tuning + month-chip polish now (deferred — each is a separate tiny follow-up to keep this PR minimal); a mobile day-view (out of scope — structural). Pinned by `tests/app/calendar/appointment-card-refresh.test.ts` (time-range formatter; card renders the range + bold name + service on its own line; `border-l-4`; palette off `-50`; rose still excluded; deterministic color stable; render-only — no query/logic/migration; no Fresha strings). No payment/Stripe/cron/intake/photo/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). Production migration remains **0097**. (Deferred visual follow-ups: open-hours band, gridline tuning, month-chip polish, mobile day-view.)

### Treatment photo UX cleanup — labels + session date (Chloe pilot feedback, no migration)

**Decision (2026-07-01):** stop showing the raw `.jpg` filename in the Treatment Photos UI, title photos with a human label (session date), and unify the confusing "Block photo" vs "Treatment area photo" mismatch — WITHOUT redesigning the photo-context model. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback):** the card title was the sanitized `original_filename` (e.g. "IMG_4821.jpg"), which is noise; and the gallery card said "Block photo" while the upload selector said "Treatment area photo" for the same scope. **Finding (inspection):** the card + modal rendered `img.filename`/`modal.filename` (from `treatment_images.original_filename`, migration 0092); the image query embedded `session_blocks (...)` (via `session_block_id`) but NOT the session's `started_at`, so the session DATE wasn't available to the UI; `treatmentPhotoScopeLabel` (`photo-context.ts`) returned "Block photo" for block scope while the selector option was "Treatment area photo". **Ponytail pass: render-only — no migration/schema/RLS, no sanitizer/storage-path/signed-URL/archive/session-attach change.** Changes: (1) `treatmentPhotoScopeLabel` block → **"Treatment area photo"** (+ the `PhotoScopeLabel` type), matching the selector — one consistent label; (2) the image query gains a **read-only embed** `sessions ( started_at )` (via the direct `treatment_images.session_id` FK — unambiguous; no schema change) and the row exposes `sessionDate`; (3) `TreatmentImagesManager` drops the `filename` Row field and renders a human title — **"Session {session date}"** when attached to a session, **"Uploaded {date}"** fallback otherwise — in both the card and the larger-preview modal; image `alt`/`aria` become "Treatment photo" (never the filename). The upload file-picker's transient "selected file" name is left as-is (user-initiated, not a stored photo). **Explicitly deferred** (per the feedback — need a migration or a product decision): per-photo notes/caption column, removing "Client photo", unifying "Session photo" vs "Treatment area photo", the full photo-context redesign. **Alternatives considered:** mapping `session_id`→date from the already-loaded upload-selector `sessionOptions` (rejected — that list is bounded/recent, wouldn't cover every gallery photo's session; the direct embed is correct + still read-only); removing "Client photo" now (rejected — out of scope, product decision). Pinned by `tests/app/clients/treatment-photo-labels.test.ts` (no `img.filename`/`modal.filename`; session-date title in card + modal; upload-date fallback; unified label = selector; read-only embed; no sanitizer/storage/signed-url/schema change) + the updated `treatment-photo-context.test.ts` ("Treatment area photo"); the other photo suites stay green. No payment/Stripe/cron/intake/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). Production migration remains **0097**.

### Smart intake resend status / expiry visibility (Chloe pilot feedback, migration 0097)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** persist a display mirror of the intake link's send/expiry so the practitioner sees accurate status (last sent, expiry, days left, whether to send a fresh link), rather than the previous best-effort heuristic. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback):** the resend button worked but the UI couldn't show when the link was sent/resent, when it expires, or how many days are left. **Finding (inspection):** intake link **expiry is stateless — it lives only inside the signed HMAC token** (`{intake_id, expires_at}`, `lib/intake/tokens.ts`), recomputed as `now + INTAKE_LINK_TTL_DAYS (14)` on every mint and **never persisted**; `client_intake_forms` had no sent-at/expires-at/count columns; `getIntakeLinkAction` (Copy link) and `resendIntakeEmailAction` (Resend email) minted a fresh token and **wrote nothing**; `started_at` never advances on resend and `requested_at` (0044) is first-issue-only + null for booking-flow rows. So an accurate "expires in N days / sent {date}" card was impossible from existing data → a small migration is required (the planning report's Option A, approved). **Migration 0097 (approved):** adds three **nullable/default-safe, backfill-safe** columns to `client_intake_forms` — `intake_link_last_sent_at timestamptz`, `intake_link_expires_at timestamptz`, `intake_link_send_count integer not null default 0`. **No raw-token/token column, no RLS change, no status-enum change, no destructive change**; the signed token stays authoritative and these are a display mirror of the most recently issued link. **Shared stamping helper** `stampIntakeLinkIssued(admin, intakeId, { emailed })` (`lib/intake/queries.ts`) — refreshes `expires_at = now+TTL` and increments `send_count` on every mint, and sets `last_sent_at` **only when the link was actually emailed**; it uses the admin client so no RLS UPDATE policy is needed, never touches `responses` (saved answers preserved), and logs stamping failures without token/PII (non-fatal). Wired at **all four mint paths**: `ensureIntakeForClient` (booking/reschedule confirmation — emailed), `resendIntakeEmailAction` (emailed after success), `getIntakeLinkAction` (Copy link — refreshes expiry + count but **not** last_sent_at, since not emailed), `requestIntakeUpdateAction` (emailed = whether it actually emailed). **Status derivation** is a pure helper `computeIntakeLinkStatus` (`lib/intake/link-status.ts`): `daysLeft`, `expired`, `closeToExpiry (≤3 days)`, `usingFallback` (legacy null rows fall back to `started_at + TTL`), and the CTA label (**Send fresh link** when expired/close, **Resend again** when `send_count ≥ 2`, else **Resend intake link**). **UI:** both surfaces (the Health & Forms overview card on `/clients/[id]` and the dedicated `/clients/[id]/intake` page) compute + pass the status to `IntakeResendCard`, which renders the normal/close/expired copy and the legacy fallback hedge. **No overclaiming** — copy says "emailed"/"sent" (the recorded send), never "delivered"/"received"/"opened"/"completed"; render-only, **no cron** (days-left is computed at render). **Alternatives considered:** a render-only no-migration version using `started_at` (rejected — inaccurate after any resend; would show a wrong countdown); an atomic-increment RPC for send_count (rejected — read-then-write is fine for this low-concurrency practitioner path, keeps the migration to 3 columns); a token-version/revocation column (out of scope — status-based revocation unchanged). Pinned by `tests/app/clients/intake-resend-status.test.ts` (migration safety; status states + button labels; every mint path stamps; copy-link doesn't claim emailed; legacy fallback; no delivery overclaim; no token/PII log) and the existing `tests/app/clients/intake-resend.test.ts` (still green). Production migration 0096 → **0097**. No payment/Stripe/cron/photo/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). **Deferred:** `sent_by`, content snapshot, delivery receipt, precare-sent tracking, inspector export/audit table, automatic/cron resend.

### Postcare sent visibility (Chloe pilot feedback D, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** surface the EXISTING postcare send timestamp more clearly rather than build a compliance audit. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback + a city inspector's comment):** a practitioner couldn't tell at a glance whether postcare was sent, and an inspector may want to review pre/postcare that was sent. **Finding:** the only persisted postcare record is `appointments.postcare_email_sent_at` + `postcare_email_send_attempts` (migration 0043); the appointment detail showed a subtle "Last sent {date}" line **only when sent** (no unsent state), and the client profile showed nothing. There is **no `sent_by`, no content snapshot, no delivery receipt** — so the system can only prove a send was *recorded*, not *delivered*. **Ponytail pass: read-only visibility over existing columns — no migration/schema/RLS, no change to `sendPostcareEmailAction`, nothing sent on render.** Changes: (1) **appointment detail** (`PostcareSendButton`) — a clear "✓ Postcare sent {date}" badge (+ "· N attempts" when >1) and an explicit **"Not sent yet."** unsent state, using the props the page already passes; (2) **per-client history** — `getAppointmentsForClientProfile` now also selects `postcare_email_sent_at` + `postcare_email_send_attempts` (existing 0043 columns; read-only), and `ClientAppointmentTimeline` renders "Postcare sent {date}" on each appointment row that has a recorded send. **No overclaiming:** all copy says "sent" (the recorded send event), never "delivered"/"received" — we do not assert delivery the system can't prove. **Explicitly deferred to a later PR** (per the feedback's "start small"): `sent_by`, content snapshot, delivery receipt, precare-sent tracking, an inspector export/audit page, and any migration-backed communication-audit table. **Alternatives considered:** building the inspector audit/report now (rejected — out of scope, would need `sent_by`/snapshot + likely a migration); a new per-client postcare query (rejected — the existing timeline query already lists the client's appointments, so adding two read-only columns is smaller and avoids a second roundtrip). Pinned by `tests/app/calendar/postcare-sent-visibility.test.ts` (sent status shown when timestamp exists; unsent state when absent; attempts only when >1; no delivery language; timeline selects the columns + renders the per-row history; send action unchanged/not called on render; no schema keyword). No payment/Stripe/cron/intake/Treatment-Photo/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). (Remaining Chloe feedback — photo UX — is a separately-scoped follow-up.)

### Settings Availability / Breaks & Blocks consolidation (Chloe pilot feedback B, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** make the Availability settings page the single home for every scheduling rule and remove the separate "Breaks & blocks" tab. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback):** settings had two adjacent, confusing tabs for related scheduling rules — `/settings/availability` (weekly hours + overrides + whole-day "Blocked time") and `/settings/calendar` "Breaks & blocks" (recurring breaks + one-off timed blocks). **Finding:** the two `calendar/` sections (`RecurringBreaksSection`, `TimedBlocksSection`) ALREADY imported their server actions from `../availability/actions` — the split was purely UI/IA, the data + actions were already centralized. **Ponytail pass: IA/render only — no DB table merge, no booking-slot change, no calendar rendering change, no migration/schema/RLS.** Changes: (1) **moved** `RecurringBreaksSection.tsx` + `TimedBlocksSection.tsx` into `app/(app)/settings/availability/` (action import `../availability/actions` → `./actions`; components otherwise unchanged) and render them on the Availability page after the existing hours/overrides/blockouts client; (2) the page now also loads `getRecurringBreakRules` + `getUpcomingTimedBlocks` and **clarifies copy** for the four concepts (weekly opening hours, whole-day blocked dates, repeating breaks, one-off timed blocks); (3) **removed** the "Breaks & blocks" nav item from `settings/layout.tsx`; (4) `/settings/calendar/page.tsx` is now a `redirect("/settings/availability")` so existing bookmarks/deep links resolve safely. The owner-only gate (`practitioner.role !== "owner"`) and the shared `availability/actions.ts` (and its studio/ownership gates) are preserved — no action behavior changed. **DB tables NOT merged:** `studio_blockouts` (whole-day) and `studio_timed_blocks` (timed, incl. all-day) remain distinct; the duplicate user-facing "block a whole day" concept is left for a future schema decision — this PR only clarifies the copy (whole-day blocked dates vs one-off timed blocks). **Alternatives considered:** keep the section files in `calendar/` and import cross-directory (rejected — the move makes Availability the genuine home, no awkward cross-dir import); a compatibility page with a "moved" notice instead of a redirect (rejected — a redirect is cleaner for deep links and leaves no stale duplicate). Pinned by `tests/app/settings/availability-consolidation.test.ts` (Availability renders both sections + loads their data; owner gate kept; sections live under `availability/` and import `./actions`; copy clarifies the four concepts; nav item removed; `/settings/calendar` redirects; no booking-slot/calendar-render/schema change). No payment/Stripe/cron/intake/Treatment-Photo/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). (Remaining Chloe feedback — postcare audit, photo UX — are separately-scoped follow-ups; the whole-day-block table merge is a future schema decision.)

### Calendar blocked-time visibility + open-hours clarity (Chloe pilot feedback B/C, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** make blocked time visible in month view, show the full-day blockout reason in week view, and make open hours readable at a glance. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback):** blocked time showed in week but **not month** view; the week full-day overlay said a generic "Blocked" (not the reason); and the open-hours treatment (a near-white gray on the closed regions) was too subtle to read the working window. **Findings:** (a) `renderMonthView` (`app/(app)/calendar/page.tsx`) loaded NO block data — `MonthView` rendered appointments only; (b) the week full-day overlay in `DayColumn.tsx` hard-coded the string "Blocked" and the page collapsed blockouts to a date `Set`, discarding `studio_blockouts.reason`; (c) the out-of-hours tint was `bg-neutral-100/80`. **Ponytail pass: calendar render only — no schema/migration, no booking-slot change, no settings IA change.** Changes: (1) **Month blocked-time** — `renderMonthView` now loads the SAME sources as the week view (`getBlockouts` + `getTimedBlocksForRange` + `getRecurringBreakOccurrencesForRange`, reusing existing range fetchers, no new query/migration), and a new pure helper `groupMonthBlockedByDate` (`app/(app)/calendar/month-blocked.ts`) produces a per-day `{ fullDay, fullDayReason, labels }` summary that `MonthView` renders as a compact muted chip (full-day reason, or first timed-block/break label `+N`); recurring breaks are skipped on closed dates to match the week view. (2) **Week blockout reason** — the page now maps each blocked date to its reason (`blockoutReasonByDate`), passes `blockedReason` to `DayColumn`, and the overlay renders `displayBlockoutLabel(reason)` (fallback "Blocked"); recurring-break (`displayRecurringBreakLabel`) and timed-block (`TIMED_BLOCK_LABEL`) labels are unchanged. (3) **Open hours** — the out-of-hours tint is a clearer-but-calm `bg-neutral-200/70` + a hairline edge (dark-mode paired), so the open window stands out without a status color. **Shared labels** (`TIMED_BLOCK_LABEL`, `displayRecurringBreakLabel`, new `displayBlockoutLabel`) were moved from `DayColumn.tsx` (a `"use client"` module) into the server-safe `calendar-format.ts` so the server-rendered month view resolves the same labels (calling a client-module function from the server throws); `DayColumn` imports them back. The grouping helper lives in a plain `.ts` (`month-blocked.ts`) so it is unit-testable without a JSX transform. **Why a compact chip (not full block cards) in month:** month cells are dense (3 appointment chips + "+N more"); a single muted chip per day keeps it calm + orientation-focused. **Alternatives considered:** a positive open-window highlight band (deferred — strengthening the closed contrast + a hairline edge was the smaller, lower-risk change); rendering full block cards in month (rejected — clutter). Pinned by `tests/app/calendar/block-visibility.test.ts` (month receives/groups block data; full-day spans + reason; timed/break labels; recurring skipped on closed; week reason vs "Blocked" fallback; clearer tint; no booking-slot/settings-IA change). No payment/Stripe/cron/intake/Treatment-Photo/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). (Other Chloe feedback — settings IA, postcare audit, photo UX — remain separately-scoped follow-ups.)

### Calendar return-to-date navigation (Chloe pilot feedback C, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** when a practitioner opens an appointment from the calendar and returns, send them back to the view/date they came from instead of resetting to today. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback):** she'd navigate to a future week, click an appointment, hit back, and land on today's week — losing her place. **Finding:** calendar view/date lives entirely in URL searchParams (`?view`/`?week`/`?month`, `app/(app)/calendar/page.tsx`), but the appointment card linked to a bare `/calendar/${id}` (no context), the detail page (`app/(app)/calendar/[id]/page.tsx`) didn't read searchParams, and its back link was hard-coded `href="/calendar"` → always the current week. **Ponytail pass: routing/render only — no schema/migration, no appointment mutation, no other calendar rendering touched.** Added a tiny pure helper `app/(app)/calendar/calendar-return.ts` (`buildCalendarReturnParams` + `calendarReturnHref`): the week page computes `?view=week&week=<weekStart>` and passes it as a `returnTo` prop to `DayColumn`, whose appointment links become `/calendar/${id}${returnTo}`; the detail page reads `view`/`week`/`month` from searchParams and builds the back link via `calendarReturnHref`. **Safety — no open-redirect:** the helper never accepts or echoes a full URL; it reconstructs `/calendar` + a validated subset (`view` ∈ {week,month}; `week`/`month` strict `YYYY-MM-DD`), dropping anything else, so the back link can only ever be internal to `/calendar` and falls back to bare `/calendar` when context is missing or hostile. **Why params, not a `returnTo` URL:** passing atomic validated params (vs a return URL) removes the open-redirect surface entirely. **Scope note:** appointment detail is reachable only from week view today (month-view chips are non-links; the day cell links to the week), so preserving the week anchor covers the real flow; direct `/calendar/<id>` links are unaffected (fallback `/calendar`). **Alternatives considered:** a single `returnTo` URL param (rejected — open-redirect validation burden); browser `referer`/history (rejected — unreliable for SSR back link). Pinned by `tests/app/calendar/return-nav.test.ts` (week/month anchor preserved; empty → `/calendar`; unknown view + malformed dates dropped; array params normalized; hostile inputs never yield an external URL; the three wiring source pins). No payment/Stripe/cron/intake/Treatment-Photo/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). (Other Chloe feedback — smart scheduling shipped (PR #298); settings IA, calendar blocked-time visibility/open-hours, postcare audit, photo UX remain separately-scoped follow-ups.)

### Smart / packed booking slots (Chloe pilot feedback A, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** make public booking availability pack the practitioner's day instead of offering every arbitrary 15-minute slot. **Why (Chloe pilot feedback, urgent):** an empty day that opens at 10:00 showed 10:00/10:15/10:30/10:45…, and slots didn't attach cleanly around existing appointments — creating inefficient gaps. **Finding:** `getAvailableSlots` (`lib/booking/slots.ts`) generated a **fixed open-anchored 15-minute grid** (`SLOT_GRANULARITY_MINUTES=15`), with existing appointments/timed-blocks/recurring-breaks/full-day-blockouts (the `studio_calendar_reservations` shadow) acting only as a subtractive overlap filter — never as packing anchors. **Ponytail pass: algorithm change in `slots.ts` only — no schema/RLS change, no migration, no new query, no change to the conflict model or the buffer/duration/DST semantics.** Candidate starts now come from three sources: **(1)** the opening time (the main opening anchor); **(2)** immediately after **each existing reservation's protected end** (`candidateMs.add(c.end)`) — the reservation's `ends_at` already bakes in the relevant buffer for appointments (migration 0029), so we do NOT add the buffer again (that was the migration-0029 double-count bug), making `c.end` the earliest legal next start; **(3)** a deliberately **coarse hourly fallback** (`FALLBACK_GRANULARITY_MINUTES=60`, generated in local minutes via `utcInstantFromLocal` per step so DST is handled exactly like the old grid) so a long empty stretch still offers a few choices rather than collapsing to a single slot. Each candidate is kept only if a full `duration` fits before close and its protected interval `[start, end + currentBuffer)` clears every conflict — the **same half-open overlap rule** as the DB gist exclusion. So an empty 10:00–17:00 day now offers 10:00, 11:00, … 16:00 (hourly), and a 10:00–11:00 appointment with a 15-min buffer offers 11:15 (end+buffer) next, not 11:00 or the 15-minute grid. **Why the anchor model, not fully greedy:** a fully-greedy pack-from-open could hide too much availability; the anchor + coarse fallback gives clean packing while preserving client choice in open windows (the user's explicit guidance). **Why no migration:** all inputs (availability, the reservation shadow, `buffer_minutes`, service duration) already exist; only the candidate-generation loop changed. Public booking display (`fetchPublicSlotsAction`), the re-verify (`publicBookAppointmentAction`), and the next-available scan all call the SAME `getAvailableSlots`, so a shown slot stays bookable and the DB exclusion/double-booking protections are untouched. **Alternatives considered:** keep the grid but widen the step (rejected — still gappy, doesn't attach to appointments); fully-greedy pack (rejected — hides availability); a per-studio "slot strategy" setting (deferred — start with the sensible default). Pinned by `tests/lib/booking/slots-smart-scheduling.test.ts` (empty-day hourly anchors not 15-min grid; after-appointment + buffer anchor; per-appointment anchors; buffer 0 vs 15; duration fit before close; block/blockout/closed respected; shared generator + no-fixed-grid source pins). No payment/Stripe/cron/intake/Treatment-Photo/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). Live payments remain disabled. (Other Chloe feedback — settings IA, calendar visibility/nav, postcare audit, photo UX — are separately-scoped follow-ups.)

### Controlled live-payment enablement prep (PR #297, no migration, docs + CI safety-lock)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** prepare for a deliberate live-payment cutover **without enabling anything** — consolidate the enablement procedure into one ordered checklist and lock the current "disabled" posture so it can't regress. **Why:** the controlled-enablement runbook already existed (docs/16 §17, PR #282) with rollback (§17.8), first-live procedure (§17.5/.9), and read-only reconciliation SQL (§17.7), but the *sequence of gates to relax* was scattered across §6/§11/§12.13/§17, and nothing failed CI if a gate were accidentally relaxed. **Finding (inspection):** live is blocked by 4 independent layers — (1) the env/key gate (`assertStripeKeyAllowed` requires `STRIPE_ALLOW_LIVE_MODE === "true"` + forbids live keys on preview/dev); (2) 8 runtime dormancy guards (charge ×2, refund ×2, receipt ×1, card-auth scoping ×2, claim-RPC mirror ×1, all driven by `inferStripeLivemode()` / the row's `stripe_livemode`); (3) the DB CHECK `payment_charge_attempts_livemode_false_check` (migration 0073) + the claim RPC mirror (0075); (4) the webhook `shouldIgnoreLiveModeEvent` which ignores every `event.livemode===true` event and does NOT consult `STRIPE_ALLOW_LIVE_MODE`. **So flipping the env flag alone does nothing** — the env flip is the LAST step after every other layer is deliberately relaxed in its own PR. **Ponytail pass: docs + a read-only CI test only — no gate touched, no env change, no migration, no Stripe call.** Added **docs/16 §17.12** (the ordered enablement checklist: business/legal → live Stripe account → live webhook → DB CHECK → claim RPC → charge/refund/receipt guards → webhook ignore → card-auth scoping → static gates → env flip last; plus the first-live + rollback cross-refs and the shipped-backstop list #281/#290/#291/#296), and **`tests/lib/billing/live-mode-disabled.test.ts`** — a source-grep safety-lock that pins every layer intact (env opt-in clause; preview/dev forbidden; the `livemode_false` DB CHECK + claim-RPC `not_ready` mirror; charge/refund `live_mode_blocked`; receipt `stripe_livemode !== false` + hardcoded `livemode: false`; card-auth `.eq("stripe_livemode", livemode)`; webhook `shouldIgnoreLiveModeEvent` + `stripe_webhook_livemode_event_ignored`; the 1/1/0/0 Stripe-gate rule declarations). **Why a CI lock, not just docs:** it makes "live got enabled accidentally" un-shippable — relaxing any gate now also requires editing this test, forcing the change to be explicit and reviewed (the real enablement PR will do both). **Alternatives considered:** an optional read-only pre-flight script (deferred — overlaps the CI test); actually relaxing a gate (explicitly out of scope — this PR enables nothing). No env change, no key rotation, no migration, no DB-CHECK/guard/webhook/card-auth relaxation. Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). **Live payments remain DISABLED; controlled live-payment enablement has not started.**

### Refund owner gate — defense in depth (PR #296, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** harden the owner-only refund boundary before live payments. **Finding:** refund initiation was **already studio-owner-only** (shipped PR #201, docs/18 §16.3) — both action callers (`refundPaymentChargeAttemptAction`, `refundFeeAttemptAction`) require `practitioner.role === "owner"` before any Stripe/DB, and the single `refunds.create` (`lib/billing/payment-refund.ts:refundPaymentChargeAttempt`) is reachable from only those two owner-gated callers; the webhook is reconcile-only and the admin manual-review page (PR #290) is read-only. **The one latent gap:** owner-only lived ONLY in the action layer — the shared helper re-checked studio scope + live-mode but **not** `role === "owner"`, so a future caller reaching the helper without the action gate could move money out as a non-owner. **Ponytail pass: helper-level defense-in-depth re-check + safe denial logging + tests/docs — no migration, no schema/RLS change, no new refund surface, no charge-auth change.** Added an owner re-check inside `refundPaymentChargeAttempt` (reads the actor's `practitioners.role` scoped to `(practitionerId, studioId)` + `active`, returns the existing `not_authorized` outcome) placed **after** the studio-scope check but **before** the claim UPDATE and the `refunds.create` — reading only the existing `practitioners.role` column (no migration). Added `logInternal("payment_refund_denied_not_owner", { studioId, practitionerId })` on both action owner-denial paths (previously silent), with **safe IDs + event name only** (no client name/email/phone, no health/treatment data, no Stripe secret/payload). **Why helper-level re-check over a CI-only guardrail:** money-OUT warrants a runtime boundary, not just a static pin, before live payments — a single scoped SELECT is cheap and cannot be bypassed by a new call site. **Why studio-owner, not platform-admin:** money-movement uses the per-studio `practitioners.role`; `ADMIN_EMAILS` is cross-studio platform read access, not the refund authority. **Alternatives considered:** CI guardrail source-grep only (rejected — weaker than a runtime check at the money boundary; kept as an ADDITIONAL test pin); consolidating the ~25 inlined `role !== "owner"` sites into a shared `assertOwner` (deferred — separate refactor); UI hiding of the refund button for non-owners (deferred — pure UX; the server gate is the real boundary). Pinned by `tests/lib/billing/payment-refund-source.test.ts` (both callers owner-gated before the helper call; helper re-checks role before the claim + `refunds.create`; exactly one `refunds.create`; denial logging carries no PII). Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). **Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement has not started.**

### Record Keeping print: disinfectant replace-by + status (PR #295, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** make the `/records/print` disinfectants section match what the in-app Record Keeping screen already shows. **Why:** PR #280 (migration 0096) added `record_keeping_disinfectants.discard_due_date` ("replace by") plus a pure read-time status helper (`lib/record-keeping/disinfectant-status.ts`: `disinfectantDueStatus` / `disinfectantStatusLabel` / `isDisinfectantAlert`), surfaced on the in-app Records screen — but the **inspector print view omitted both**, so a printed disinfectant log was silent on whether a batch was past its replace-by date. An inspector handed the printout couldn't see the overdue/due status the screen shows. **Ponytail pass: render-only, reuse the shipped helpers, no migration, no query/schema/RLS change.** In `app/(app)/records/print/page.tsx`, `DisinfectantsPrint` now takes the studio `timezone`, computes the deterministic studio-local `today` via the same `todayInTz` the in-app screen uses, and renders a **"Replace by"** line (`discard_due_date` or "Not set") plus a **"Replace status"** line (`disinfectantStatusLabel`) for overdue/due-today/due-soon batches (via `isDisinfectantAlert`). `getDisinfectantRecords` already `select("*")`s `discard_due_date`/`date_discarded`, so **no query change** — purely display. **Optional add-ons were intentionally skipped** because they would require a query/schema change (against scope): the sterile-items "recorded-by" operator NAME (only `created_by_practitioner_id` is available, not a name — would need a practitioners join) and the procedure-print "probe lot confirmed" flag (`probe_lot_confirmed` isn't in the `getClientProcedureRecords` select). **Alternatives considered:** combined all-sections inspection packet, ZIP-export inclusion of the record-keeping logbooks, history-on-by-default, real-PDF/signature — all explicitly deferred (separate, larger PRs). Pinned by `tests/app/records/print-export.test.ts` (source-grep: references `discard_due_date`, renders "Replace by", uses the three helpers + `todayInTz(timezone)`, render-only with no `.insert/.update/.upsert/.delete` and no schema/RLS DDL). The pure status logic stays covered by `tests/lib/record-keeping/disinfectant-status.test.ts`. No cron/reminder, no payment/Stripe, no intake/Treatment-Photo change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Intake link resend / refresh — overview-card visibility follow-up (PR #293 hotfix, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** render the resend CTA on the surface practitioners actually use. **Production verification of #293 found the CTA was invisible for a real in-progress client (Angelina):** the practitioner-facing "Health & Forms" tab is the inline **`activeTab === "health"` "Health intake" card on `app/(app)/clients/[id]/page.tsx`** (the client overview), but #293 added the CTA only to the *separate* `app/(app)/clients/[id]/intake/page.tsx`. For an `in_progress` intake the overview card showed only "Intake started …, not yet submitted." with no link to the dedicated page and no CTA — so Chloe never reached it. **Placement bug, not a cache issue** (the deployed SHA matched; static analysis showed the CTA was never on the overview surface; read-only DB confirmed Angelina's single intake is `in_progress`). **Smallest fix:** render the existing `IntakeResendCard` inside the overview card's `intake?.status === "in_progress"` branch (`clientId`/`intakeId={intake.id}`/`clientHasEmail`/`linkMaybeExpired` from `INTAKE_LINK_TTL_DAYS` + `intake.started_at`) + a trivial behavior-neutral "View intake →" link (parity with the submitted/reviewed states). **Reuses the existing component + backend actions verbatim — no new action, no token-model/TTL/route/submit change, no migration; single file `page.tsx` (+ test).** Pinned by the extended `tests/app/clients/intake-resend.test.ts` (overview page imports + renders `IntakeResendCard` inside the in_progress branch, wired to `intake.id`). Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Intake link resend / refresh — discoverability fix (PR #293, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** give Chloe a prominent, clearly-labeled **"Resend intake link"** button on the client Health & Forms tab. **Why (pilot blocker):** client Angelina's intake link expired and Chloe saw no obvious way to send a fresh one without admin friction. **Inspection finding:** the capability already existed but was hidden — `resendIntakeEmailAction` (mints a fresh 14-day token for the *same* intake row, preserving saved answers via the `responses` JSONB, and re-emails) was a small per-row "Resend email" button buried in the "Intake history" list, while the prominent card was "Request intake update" (`requestIntakeUpdateAction`), which starts a brand-new *blank* intake — the wrong tool, and the tab gave no "expired link" cue. The token is a stateless HMAC (`{intake_id, expires_at}` signed with `INTAKE_SIGNING_SECRET`; no `token`/`expires_at` DB column), so re-minting a link is inherently a fresh 14-day window with no DB write. **Ponytail pass: pure UX/discoverability — reuse the existing backend actions verbatim, no migration, no token-model/TTL/route/submit change, no new server action.** Added `app/(app)/clients/[id]/intake/IntakeResendCard.tsx` (a primary CTA calling the existing `resendIntakeEmailAction` on `intake.id` + a `getIntakeLinkAction` Copy-link fallback), rendered on `page.tsx` only when the current intake is `in_progress`, above the now-secondary card; relabeled `IntakeReissueCard` from "Request intake update" → **"Send a new intake form"** (helper: "Starts a brand-new blank intake. Use Resend intake link if the client already started this form."); and exported the existing `INTAKE_LINK_TTL_DAYS = 14` so the page can show a best-effort "the previous link may have expired" hint when an in-progress intake is older than the TTL. Copy: helper "Sends the client a fresh secure link and keeps any answers they've already saved. Links expire after 14 days."; success "Intake link sent."; error "Could not send the intake link. Please try again."; no-email "No email on file — use Copy link to share it manually." **Why not the reissue card:** it discards started progress (new blank row) — the opposite of what an expired-link-to-an-unsubmitted-form needs. **Why no precise "expired" badge:** the app doesn't store link mint/send time (expiry is token-side); a precise badge would need a `last_intake_link_sent_at` column (migration) — deferred; the age-based hint is best-effort and needs no schema. **Alternatives considered:** extending/removing the TTL (rejected — security; keep 14 days + fresh-token-on-resend), a token-version/revoke column (separate future work), reusing expired tokens (rejected). Pinned by `tests/app/clients/intake-resend.test.ts` (source-grep: CTA → `resendIntakeEmailAction` same row, NOT `requestIntakeUpdateAction`; copy keeps-answers + 14-day; no-email→Copy-link; relabeled secondary card; no `console.`/ops-alert/token/PII logging). Studio-member gating, RLS, the public `/intake/<token>` route, submit/save, and the token model are all unchanged. No Stripe/payment/cron/reminder/Treatment-Photo/Record-Keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Treatment image upload pre/post-buffer size hardening (PR #292, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** add two defense-in-depth byte-length guards to the Treatment Photo upload action — Treatment Photos reliability/security hardening. **Why:** the pipeline is already strong (real `instanceof File` guard, MIME allowlist + `file.size > 0` + `file.size <= 15 MB` **before** `arrayBuffer()`, then the authoritative Sharp sanitizer — real-format detect, 100 MP pixel-bomb limit, fake-MIME guard, EXIF/GPS/XMP/ICC strip, sanitized bytes stored, no public URLs), but inspection found two gaps, both **after `arrayBuffer()`**: (1) the 15 MB cap trusted the **client-reported `file.size`** and never re-bound the **actual buffered length**; (2) the **re-encoded sanitizer output** was never byte-capped (bounded only by the 100 MP pixel guard, so a re-encode that grows past 15 MB would still upload). For a real server-action `File` these are low-risk (buffered length == `file.size`), so this is **defense-in-depth, not a live exploit**. **Ponytail pass: two guards in `uploadTreatmentImageAction` only — no sanitizer/storage/signed-URL/bucket/UI change, no migration, no new constant.** Guard (1): after `arrayBuffer()` and **before** the sanitizer, re-run `validateTreatmentImageUpload({ contentType: valid.contentType, sizeBytes: inputBytes.byteLength })` — reusing the single-source validator + `TREATMENT_IMAGE_MAX_BYTES` + the same generic strings — so an over-cap/empty **actual** buffer is rejected before any Sharp work.
