Guard (2): after the sanitizer and **before** the storage upload, `if (sanitized.bytes.byteLength > TREATMENT_IMAGE_MAX_BYTES) return generic "Could not upload the image."`. **Why re-use `validateTreatmentImageUpload` rather than a new check:** single source of truth for the cap + error strings; the buffered-length re-validation is literally the same gate applied to the real bytes. **Why not a strict `byteLength === file.size` equality reject:** platform `File` implementations can differ; enforcing the byte length **independently** (`> 0`, `<= max`) is the safe, false-positive-free choice the task called for. **Why not streaming upload / a `next.config` body-limit change:** the framework ceiling is already `bodySizeLimit: "16mb"` (just above the cap) and the pre-buffer `file.size` gate already bounds memory; an independent post-buffer re-check is sufficient and far smaller. **Alternatives considered:** buffered-length re-check only (rejected — leaves the output-growth gap); a new dedicated byte-only validator (rejected — duplicates the constant/strings); a migration / schema change (none needed); touching the sanitizer (rejected — it is authoritative and correct). Pinned by `tests/app/clients/treatment-image-upload-bounds.test.ts` (behavioral validator bounds + real-Sharp valid-image-output-within-cap + source-grep ordering: `file.size` before `arrayBuffer()`, buffered `byteLength` before the sanitizer, sanitized-output cap before `.upload(`, sanitized-not-original bytes stored, no `getPublicUrl`, no `.withMetadata()`). The existing Treatment Photos suites (sanitizer, storage hardening, archive scope, attach context, UI) stay green. No storage/bucket/signing/sanitizer/RLS/calendar/payment/charting/record-keeping/reminder change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). **Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement has not started.**

### Critical ops-alert delivery verification (PR #291, no migration, env gate + tests + docs)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** make critical ops-alert delivery configuration **explicit, testable, and production-gated** — operations reliability hardening before live payments. **Why:** since PR #193, critical ops alerts (payment / storage / cron / webhook failures) email the recipients in `OPS_ALERT_EMAILS` via `lib/ops/alert-email.ts`, but that env var was never *enforced* — a production deploy with `OPS_ALERT_EMAILS` unset/empty makes the email a silent no-op, so a critical failure would exist only as a DB row + `/admin/ops-alerts` and operators might not see it in time. **Ponytail pass: production env gate + tests + docs only — no migration, no new UI, no new alert provider, no real alert send, no runtime change to `alert-email.ts`/`alerts.ts` (already gated-to-critical, after-the-durable-row, never-throws, redacted, no-op-with-warn).** Extended `scripts/check-production-env-gates.mjs` (the PR #262 build-time gate, wired into `npm run build`) with a **second gate**: when `VERCEL_ENV === "production"`, require `OPS_ALERT_EMAILS` to parse to **≥1 recipient** — mirroring `parseOpsAlertEmails` (`split(",")`/trim/filter) via a new `opsAlertRecipientCount()` so whitespace-only / comma-only (which deliver to nobody) also fail. Own PASS/FAIL label `ops-alert-delivery-env`, **names-only output (the configured addresses are never printed)**, both gates run and the build exits non-zero if either fails. **Why ≥1-recipient, not just non-empty:** a `length>0` check would pass a `" , "` value that the runtime treats as zero recipients — a residual silent-misconfig hole; mirroring the runtime parser closes it. **Why a build-gate, not a runtime change:** the alert path is already robust; the only gap is that misconfig can *ship* — a deploy-time gate is exactly the deploy-time complement (same pattern as the PR #262 Upstash gate). **Outside production it stays optional** (the gate SKIPs on local/CI/preview, keying on `VERCEL_ENV`, so dev needs no alert email). **Alternatives considered:** non-empty-only validation (rejected — whitespace/comma-only hole); folding `OPS_ALERT_EMAILS` into the existing Upstash required-list (rejected — the Upstash PASS/FAIL text is Upstash-specific and would mislabel it; a separate gate label is clearer); adding Slack/PagerDuty/OpsGenie/`CRITICAL_OPS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` or an alert-delivery health check or an admin "send test alert" button now (all **deferred** — out of scope; a real send/new provider is more than needed for "don't ship misconfigured"); a migration (none — config + docs only). Also corrected two **factually-stale docs** that predated PR #193: docs/10's `OPS_ALERT_EMAILS` row ("Reserved (not read today) … Leave unset") and docs/11's "email dispatch is deliberately not implemented … not read" — both now state it is read since #193 and **required in production** since #291. Pinned by `tests/scripts/check-production-env-gates.test.ts` (spawn cases: prod+missing/empty/whitespace→FAIL/exit 1, prod+configured→PASS/exit 0, off-prod→SKIP, address-never-printed, gates independent; source-grep: `OPS_ALERT_EMAILS` + `ops-alert-delivery-env` + `opsAlertRecipientCount`, no email-send/external-provider/Stripe-gate/bypass added). No runtime/payment/reminder/manual-review change; Stripe gates unchanged (1/1/0/0). **Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement has not started.**

### Payment manual-review queue (PR #290, no migration, read-only admin)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** give operators a focused, read-only in-app surface for payment attempts that need review before live payments. **Why:** PR #281 made `needs_manual_review` a real outcome + critical ops alert, but it was not an operator *workflow* — staff had to eyeball the mixed `/admin/ops-alerts` list or run the docs/16 §17.7 SQL in the Supabase console (docs/18 §6/§11 flagged "no payment admin tool" as part of the P0 ops gap). **This explicitly supersedes the PR #282 entry's "no new admin UI" stance:** PR #282 *deferred* the queue (documented read-only SQL + the existing ops-alerts page were the interim); PR #290 is that deferred dedicated queue. **Ponytail pass: read-only, no migration, no payment mutation, no Stripe call, reuse the existing admin gate, derive from existing rows/alerts.** Added `lib/billing/payment-manual-review.ts` (pure, dependency-free selection + safe view-models — no server-only/DB/Stripe) and the read-only page `app/admin/payments/manual-review/page.tsx` (admin-only via the `app/admin` `isAdmin` layout gate + a defensive `getUser`+`isAdmin`→`notFound` page re-check; `force-dynamic`; **service-role read** so `studio_id`-NULL alerts are visible, mirroring `/admin/ops-alerts`), plus a `/admin` QuickLink. Two derived sections **mirror docs/16 §17.7 queries (1) and (3)/(6)**: (1) `payment_charge_attempts` `status='pending_stripe'` AND `updated_at < now()-60min`; (2) `ops_alerts` `severity='critical'` AND `resolved_at IS NULL`, then a pure `selectPaymentReviewAlerts` keeps only the payment manual-review event prefixes (`session_payment_` / `payment_intent_` / `payment_refund_` / `payment_charge_` / `charge_refunded_` / `stripe_webhook_`). **Critical-only by decision** — warning-level reconciliation alerts (no_match, `*_reconcile_zero_rows`, livemode-event-ignored) and `card_on_file_setup_failed` stay on the full `/admin/ops-alerts` list, so the queue is operator-actionable payment risk, not a second ops inbox. **Privacy:** displays only safe fields (studio name + ids, `client_id`/`session_id`/`appointment_id` **ids — NO client names**, amount/currency, Hone `status`, `charge_reason`, `failure_code`, the non-secret `stripe_payment_intent_id`, a live/test-mode flag, the alert event/severity, and the **redacted-at-write** alert message); the view-model mappers copy a fixed allowlist so a row carrying a client name / notes / `failure_message_safe` / card data / `stripe_customer_id` can never leak. **Read-only:** no resolve/retry/refund/repair button, no payment-attempt mutation, no Stripe API call — resolution stays on the existing `/admin/ops-alerts` resolve action (the queue links there). A failed DB read throws a **generic** error (no provider-message leak, PR #285 posture) rather than rendering a misleading empty "all clear" queue (caught by an adversarial review pass). **No migration** — `payment_charge_attempts` + `ops_alerts` already carry every field (there is no `needs_manual_review` status; the row stays `pending_stripe`). **Alternatives considered:** extend `/admin/ops-alerts` with a payment filter (rejected — it is alert-centric and mixed; the queue is attempt-centric and joins the stuck ledger rows); include warnings (rejected — noise; chosen critical-only); add a resolve/retry button here (rejected — read-only this PR; resolution stays on ops-alerts; retry/refund are explicitly out of scope and gated by the docs/16 §17 runbook); a migration adding a `needs_manual_review` status/column (rejected — unnecessary; derivable). Pinned by `tests/app/admin/payment-manual-review.test.ts` (behavioral pure-helper selection + no-PII view-models + source-grep: admin-gate inherited + re-check, force-dynamic, service-role read, the two §17.7 queries, no mutation, no Stripe write, no client name, loud-on-read-error, links to ops-alerts). No charge/refund/webhook/payment-success behavior change; Stripe gates unchanged (1 `paymentIntents.create` / 1 `refunds.create` / 0 `charges.create` / 0 `checkout.sessions`). **Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement has not started.**

### Calendar feed privacy hardening (PR #289, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** make the iCal subscription feed privacy-preserving by default. **Finding (fresh ZIP review, P2):** the feed URL `/calendar-feed/<token>.ics` is a **bearer secret** that third-party calendar providers (Google / Apple / Outlook) store along with the event contents — and the default ICS `DESCRIPTION` carried `Client: <client name>` + `Type: <modality>` (electrolysis/laser), so subscribing leaked sensitive appointment context (who, and what kind of treatment) outside Hone to anyone holding the URL. **Ponytail pass: default ICS output change only — no migration, no header/middleware/token change, no new UI.** The route's appointment SELECT was narrowed from `id, starts_at, ends_at, status, service:services(modality), client:clients(name)` to **`id, starts_at, ends_at`** (it no longer fetches the client name or modality at all — defense-in-depth), and each event's `DESCRIPTION` is now a generic constant ("Appointment scheduled in Hone. Open Hone for details.") + the existing auth-gated `View in Hone: <origin>/calendar/<id>` deep link (no token). `SUMMARY` stays the already-generic "Hone appointment"; `UID` stays `<appointment_id>@hone.care` (stable, no PII); `LOCATION` stays the studio's already-public address; start/end/timezone formatting unchanged; cancelled appointments stay excluded (the status WHERE filter is kept even though status is no longer projected). The now-unused `modalityLabel` / `statusDescriptor` helpers were removed. **Headers unchanged** — `next.config.ts` already applies `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` + `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` to `/calendar-feed/:token*` and the route sets `Cache-Control: private, no-store`; the hash-at-rest token lookup (PR #182, migration 0079) is untouched. **No migration:** this is a pure output change; the hash column already exists and existing feed tokens keep working (no format/hash change). **Why default-private rather than a setting:** the leak is in the *default* a practitioner gets the moment they subscribe; an explicit per-studio "include client names" opt-in is the right future enhancement but should not be the default — so it's **deferred** (along with token rotation/revoke UI + last-used telemetry). **Alternatives considered:** keeping client names but adding a setting to opt OUT (rejected — privacy must be the default, not opt-out); removing the feed (rejected — the brief forbids it and the feature is useful for busy/free times). Pinned by `tests/app/calendar-feed/feed-privacy.test.ts` (behavioral: planted client name/email/phone/address/notes/modality/token absent from the rendered ICS; generic summary/description; PII-free UID; correct times; too-short token 404s; source-grep: no `clients(name)`/`services(modality)` SELECT, generic description). Existing `route-hash-lookup.test.ts` pins remain green. No booking/reminder/payment/Treatment-Photos/charting change; Stripe gates unchanged. **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Notification test / CI reliability checkpoint (PR #288, no migration, test-only)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** investigate a previously-reported flaky/timed-out test — `tests/lib/notifications/practitioner-notifications.test.ts > runtime: invalid event type does not throw > returns void synchronously and swallows the misuse` (it timed out in the full suite but passed when rerun alone) — and de-flake it without touching production. **Root cause (test-isolation, not a code bug):** the invalid-event smoke calls `recordPractitionerNotification` with a bad `eventType`, which runs the helper's `fireAndForgetOpsAlert` — an **unawaited** `void (async () => { await import("@/lib/ops/alerts"); await recordOpsAlert(...) })()`. The test asserted the synchronous `void` return but left that dynamic-import + `createAdminClient` chain pending in the background; under full-suite `pool: "forks"` contention that dangling work (heavy module load + admin-client) is the plausible source of the occasional timeout. **The fire-and-forget never-throws contract is correct production behavior** (a notification miss must never roll back the committed booking/cancel/reschedule), so no production code changed. **Ponytail pass: test-only, no migration, no production change.** The runtime smoke now `vi.mock`s the fire-and-forget targets (`@/lib/ops/alerts` `recordOpsAlert` → no-op; `@/lib/supabase/admin-server` `createAdminClient` → throws, exercising the swallow path) so the background work is a controlled no-op, **flushes microtasks** (`setTimeout(0)`) so the chain settles INSIDE the test (no open handle past the boundary), and adds deterministic assertions: invalid event → returns `undefined`, doesn't throw, logs **exactly one** `practitioner_notification_invalid_event_type` warning ops alert, and **never** calls `createAdminClient` (no insert attempted); a valid event → returns void and its mocked-admin-client throw is swallowed + reported via `practitioner_notification_insert_threw` (never surfaced to the caller). **Reproduction:** the flake did NOT reproduce (target test 5/5 + 10/10 alone, related ops/notification suites green, `npm test` green twice) — consistent with the brief's note that recent CI is green; the value is making the named test deterministic-by-construction rather than relying on un-mocked fire-and-forget timing. **Safety:** the repro is prod-write-safe because vitest loads no `.env.local` and `createAdminClient` throws on the missing `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, so the fire-and-forget `recordOpsAlert` can never reach prod. **Alternatives considered:** changing the helper to await/await-able (rejected — breaks the intended never-throws fire-and-forget contract and would extend the caller's await chain); deleting the runtime smoke (rejected — it covers a real misuse path). No migration; no notification/reminder/cron/ops-alert/payment behavior change; Stripe gates unchanged. **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Treatment image archive scope + zero-row handling (PR #287, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** make `archiveTreatmentImageAction` change **exactly the current client's row** and only then report success. **Finding (fresh ZIP review, P2):** the archive scoped its conditional UPDATE by `id + studio_id` only (not `client_id`) and never confirmed a row was changed — so a Client A route call with Client B's (same-studio) image id archived the **wrong client's** photo (RLS scopes by studio, and 0093 only freezes identity columns, so a same-studio `deleted_at` flip on another client's row is a legitimate DB update), and a nonexistent / already-archived / wrong-client id updated **zero rows** yet returned `{ ok: true }`. **Ponytail pass: archive action only, no migration, no storage/signing/sanitizer/RLS/UI change.** The UPDATE is now scoped by `id + studio_id + client_id + deleted_at IS NULL` and proves a row changed via `.select("id")`; a zero-row (or null-data) result returns a generic `{ ok: false, error: "Treatment photo not found." }` — it never revalidates the page, never reports success, and never reveals whether another client's image exists. The DB-error path keeps the existing generic "Could not archive the image." (no provider-message leak). `clientId` was already passed by the manager (`onArchive`) and was previously used only for `revalidatePath`. **Why no migration:** 0092/0093 protect storage/path/identity integrity but cannot constrain which route client an archive *action* targets — that is a pure app-level scoping gap; the archive action is the only by-id metadata mutation (upload is a client-scoped insert since PR #284; signing is a read). **Alternatives considered:** an RLS client-scoped policy / DB trigger (rejected — RLS is studio-grained by design, and a same-studio archive of another client's row is a valid DB write; the misuse is at the action layer); revealing a specific "wrong client" error (rejected — the generic not-found avoids confirming another client's image exists). Pinned by `tests/app/clients/treatment-image-archive-scope.test.ts` (behavioral mocked-Supabase: valid succeeds with the `id+studio_id+client_id` scope; zero-row/null → generic not-found, no revalidate, no success; DB error generic; source-grep on the scope + `.select("id")` + storage-unchanged). No upload/signing/storage/charting/record-keeping/payment/reminder change; Stripe gates unchanged; no public URL change. **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Clinical lineage enforcement for charting actions (PR #286, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** make charting mutations enforce the full `studio → client → session → block → entry` lineage. **Finding (fresh ZIP review, P1):** the `block-actions.ts` charting actions validated session∈**studio** (`assertSessionInStudio`) but not session∈**client**; migration 0094 enforces same-studio parent consistency (block∈session / entry∈block∈session) but does not stop a same-studio **wrong-client write** — a Client A route action submitted with Client B's (same-studio) `sessionId`/`blockId`/`entryId` (stale tab / bug / tampered form) would be accepted and corrupt treatment memory. **Ponytail pass: shared app-level assertion helper, no migration, no UI/charting change.** Added `lib/sessions/session-lineage.ts` exporting `assertSessionForClient(studioId, clientId, sessionId)` (queries `sessions` by `id + studio_id + client_id + deleted_at null`; throws `SessionLineageError("Treatment session not found.")` — generic, never leaking the provider message or whether another client/session exists), and swapped it into all **6** `block-actions.ts` actions (create/update/copy/soft-delete block + create/update treatment-area-with-entry), each of which already carries `input.clientId`. **Why session-level only is necessary AND sufficient:** every block/entry write is already scoped by `session_id` (+ `studio_id`/`block_id`), and 0094 guarantees block∈session / entry∈block∈session — so validating session∈client makes the whole chain client-correct without re-querying each level. The session-level `actions.ts` (entry add/delete, note/price/performer/started-at/soft-delete) was **already** client-aware via `assertSessionVisible` and is unchanged; the session page already loads the session client-scoped + `notFound()`, but a direct action call bypasses the page, which is exactly the gap this closes. **No migration:** 0094 supplies the structural backstop; this is the missing app-level route-client check. **Alternatives considered:** add explicit `assertBlockForClientSession` / `assert…EntryForClientSessionBlock` query helpers that re-validate each level before write (rejected — redundant DB round-trips; the existing session-scoped `.eq()` writes + 0094 already enforce block∈session / entry∈block∈session); a DB trigger/migration (rejected — 0094 already covers the structural side; the wrong-client risk is purely app-level action misuse). Defense-in-depth ABOVE RLS (RLS stays the primary studio gate); valid same-client writes are unaffected. Pinned by `tests/app/sessions/clinical-lineage.test.ts` (behavioral helper rejection + source-grep wiring across all 6 actions). No payment / Treatment-Photos / reminder / record-keeping change; Stripe gates unchanged. **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Central ops-alert message redaction (PR #285, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-30):** make every operator-alert MESSAGE safe by default. **Finding (fresh ZIP review):** `recordOpsAlert` redacted `safe_details` but only **truncated** the free-text `message`; ~30+ call sites pass a provider `error.message` (Supabase / Stripe / storage / Twilio / cron / app exceptions), which can leak PII, signed URLs, storage paths, tokens, Stripe object secrets, or DB internals into the stderr log, the `ops_alerts.message` column, the admin Ops-alerts page, AND the critical operator email. **Ponytail pass: ONE central fix, not 30+ call-site edits, no migration, no new UI, no new channel.** Added a pure, dependency-free `lib/ops/redact.ts` with `redactOpsAlertMessage(message)` (free-text scrubber) + `redactOpsAlertValue`/`redactOpsAlertDetails` (the safe_details redactor, moved here); `recordOpsAlert` now computes `message = truncate(redactOpsAlertMessage(input.message))`. Because that single `message` local feeds all four sinks, the one call protects every caller — **no per-call-site change is required for correctness**. The scrubber removes emails, phones (≥8 digits), `Bearer`/`authorization` tokens, `CRON_SECRET`, JWTs, Supabase/S3 signed URLs (`/object/sign/`), sensitive URL query params (`token`/`signature`/`X-Amz-Signature`/`expires`/`download`/`sig`), appointment/intake/manage/cancel URL tokens, `treatment-images/…` + raw `<uuid>/<uuid>/…` storage paths, Stripe `sk_`/`rk_` keys + `whsec_` + `pi_/seti_ …_secret_…` client secrets, named `token`/`secret`/`client_secret`/`password`/`api_key` fields, and any ≥32-char high-entropy token; it **preserves** non-secret Stripe object ids (`pi_`/`ch_`/`re_`/`cus_`/… not `_secret_`) and standalone UUIDs (via a protect-and-restore pass so the phone/high-entropy sweeps can't eat UUID digit runs) so operators keep reconciliation/support context. Deterministic + idempotent; the typed columns (`studio_id`/`client_id`/`stripe_payment_intent_id`/`stripe_event_id`/`route`/`event`/`severity`) are untouched. **Call-site hygiene (minimal, not a refactor):** the highest-risk storage-error alerts in `app/(app)/clients/[id]/images/actions.ts` (upload / metadata-insert / orphan-cleanup / sign) now pass generic event-descriptive messages instead of the raw provider `error.message`; everything else relies on central redaction. **safe_details** is unchanged in spirit (credential-named keys + whole-value secret shapes) and now also scrubs embedded patterns in string values; the existing `redactSafeDetails` name is preserved as an alias so prior tests stay green (one truncation-fixture test updated: a 2000-char single-token run is now correctly treated as a high-entropy token, so the fixture uses space-separated words to exercise the pure truncation path). **Alternatives considered:** editing all 30+ call sites to pass generic messages (rejected — massive refactor, and a future call site would re-introduce the leak; central redaction is leak-proof by construction); dropping the message entirely (rejected — operators lose triage context; targeted scrubbing keeps safe context). No migration / schema / RLS / payment / reminder / Treatment-Photos behavior change; Stripe gates unchanged; no new alert UI or notification channel; no public/client exposure. Pinned by `tests/lib/ops/redact.test.ts` (29 cases) + the existing `tests/lib/ops/alerts.test.ts`. **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Attach Treatment Photos to session / block at upload (PR #284, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-29):** make Treatment Photos part of the treatment-memory workflow by letting a practitioner attach a photo to a specific **session** and/or **session block / treatment area** at upload time. **Ponytail pass: no migration, no storage/RLS/signed-URL rewrite, no annotation/comparison/thumbnails/bulk/public-exposure — populate the already-existing nullable `session_id` / `session_block_id` columns (migration 0092) with server-validated ids, reusing the PR #274 context tags.** The storage/security boundary (PR #276/#277) is untouched: private bucket, service-mediated storage, byte-sanitizer + EXIF strip, path validation, orphan cleanup, no public URLs. **Change (3 files):** `actions.ts` reads `sessionId`/`sessionBlockId` from the form and **validates them server-side** before storing — session ∈ studio+client; block ∈ its session + studio, with the session **derived from the block row** so a mismatched `(block, session)` pair can never be stored; cross-tenant/invalid ids return a generic "not available for this client" error (no interpolated id) and nothing is attached; default stores both null (client photo). `page.tsx` loads recent sessions (bounded to 12, embedding each session's blocks' area fields) via the **studio+client-scoped RLS client** and passes id+label options to the manager. `TreatmentImagesManager.tsx` adds a mobile-friendly "Photo context" selector (Client / Session / Treatment area) with a client-only fallback when the client has no sessions, area labels (`sessionBlockOptionLabel`), and a "Will attach as: …" summary; ids reach the client only as `<option>` values, never as visible text. **Why no migration:** the columns already exist (0092, nullable) and the **0093 `enforce_treatment_image_integrity` trigger already enforces parent consistency** (client∈studio / session∈studio+client / block∈session+studio) as the structural backstop — the app validation is defense-in-depth + clean errors. **Scope:** Treatment-Photos-page selector only; an in-charting "attach here" entry point was considered and **deferred** (more charting-page surface; the core value lands with the Photos-page selector). **Alternatives considered:** a new attach table / migration (rejected — the columns + trigger already exist); trusting client-submitted ids and relying solely on the trigger (rejected — app validation gives clean errors + defense-in-depth). Pinned by `tests/app/clients/treatment-photo-attach.test.ts` (22 source-grep pins: stores validated ids, default client-photo, studio+client session scope, block-derives-session, cross-tenant rejection with generic error, sanitizer/path/orphan-cleanup unchanged, no public URL, page RLS-scoped read, selector UI + fallback + labels, no raw ids/paths rendered); the existing `tests/db/treatment-image-hardening.db.test.ts` proves the trigger backstop; PR #271–#277 image tests + PR #274 context-tag tests remain green. Stripe gates unchanged. **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Reminder scheduler alerting + runbook (PR #283, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-29):** make a stale/missing external reminder scheduler **actionable**, not just a passive admin card. **Ponytail pass: no new scheduler, no new cron route, no migration, no new UI, no email/SMS reminder change — a small deduped ops-alert function wired onto the EXISTING daily cron, plus docs.** PR #265 added the Upstash heartbeat + `/admin` **Reminder scheduler** card (healthy ≤45 min / stale / missing), but the signal was passive (operators only saw it if they opened `/admin`). PR #283 adds `recordReminderSchedulerHealthAlert()` to `lib/cron/reminder-heartbeat.ts`: it reads the heartbeat, classifies via the existing pure `computeReminderSchedulerStatus`, and records ONE deduped ops alert — `reminder_scheduler_stale` (**warning**) or `reminder_scheduler_missing` (**critical**, which emails `OPS_ALERT_EMAILS`). **Dedupe** = skip if an UNRESOLVED `ops_alerts` row already exists for the same event (reuses the existing `resolved_at` model + the admin resolve action; no time-window guessing, no migration). **No auto-resolve** (no existing safe pattern; operator resolves manually on `/admin/ops-alerts` after fixing the scheduler). **Where it runs:** the **existing daily `materialize-recurring-breaks` cron** (`0 8 * * *`) calls it best-effort after its own work — that cron fires automatically and **independently** of the dead external every-15-min scheduler, so a stale/missing scheduler is detected without a new job/route. The check **never sends reminders** and never calls `/api/cron/appointment-reminders`; a check failure is wrapped so it can never turn a successful materialization into `cron_route_failed`. `safe_details` are timing-only (`status`, `last_success_at`, `age_minutes`, `cadence_minutes`, `stale_after_minutes`, `checked_at`) — never `CRON_SECRET`, Authorization header, client PII, reminder content, or provider payload. **Why the daily cron, not the admin render:** writing an ops alert during a normal `/admin` GET render is a side-effect-on-read anti-pattern and would only fire when an operator is already looking (defeating "no longer silent"); the daily cron fires automatically. **Tradeoff accepted:** detection latency up to ~24h (daily cadence); the always-on admin card remains the **real-time read-only** view. **Alternatives considered:** (a) write-on-admin-render — rejected (side-effect on read; only fires when someone looks); (b) an explicit admin "check now" button — rejected for this PR (only fires on click; deferred); (c) a new sub-daily Vercel cron — rejected (plan caps cron at daily; a new job is more than needed). Pinned by `tests/lib/cron/reminder-heartbeat.test.ts` (pure decision: healthy→none, stale→1 warning, missing→1 critical, dedupe suppresses; safe_details keys are timing-only with no secret/PII) and `tests/app/cron/reminder-heartbeat-wiring.test.ts` (daily cron calls it best-effort after the auth gate; never sends reminders / calls the reminder route; dedupes on `resolved_at IS NULL`; no auto-resolve; admin page does not write-on-render). Reminder send/timing, cron auth, the heartbeat write, the admin card, and the Stripe gates are unchanged. **Live payments remain disabled.**

### Payment reconciliation + controlled live-payment readiness runbook (PR #282, no migration, docs-only)

**Decision (2026-06-29):** prepare for *future* controlled live-payment enablement by adding reconciliation checks + an operator runbook — **readiness only, NOT enablement.** **Ponytail pass: docs + read-only (SELECT-only) reconciliation SQL + one source-grep doc test — no migration, no runtime/charge/refund/webhook behavior change, no new admin UI, no executable script that connects to production, no Stripe key/env change.** Reuses the existing admin **Ops alerts** page (`/admin/ops-alerts`, PR #193 — already surfaces every critical payment alert, unresolved-critical-first, with PaymentIntent ids), the existing `payment_charge_attempts` / `ops_alerts` / `stripe_events` (migration 0032 `claim_stripe_event`) tables, the PR #179 webhook reconciliation backstop, and the PR #281 authoritative-success criticals. **Change:** added **docs/16 §17** — the authoritative post-#281 reconciliation + controlled-enablement runbook (current status: live disabled, #281 complete; required gates; Before/During/After first-live-payment checklist; forbidden actions without owner approval — no `STRIPE_ALLOW_LIVE_MODE=true`, no live keys, no live charges, no broad card-required flows; rollback plan; webhook replay procedure; and six read-only reconciliation SQL queries — stuck `pending_stripe`, Stripe-PI-present-but-local-not-succeeded, the #281 `session_payment_succeeded_write_*` criticals, refund-review alerts, unprocessed/unmapped `stripe_events`, recent payment criticals). Also **fixed the stale docs/16 §7.1/§7.2** references to the deleted `lib/billing/manual-fee-charge.ts` / `manual_fee_charge_attempts` (now `session-payment-charge.ts` / `payment_charge_attempts`), and added pointers in docs/06, docs/10, docs/12, docs/14, docs/18. **Why docs + read-only SQL over an executable reconciliation script:** the machine is production-connected via `.env.local`, so a runnable script risks a prod connection; documented SELECT-only snippets the operator runs deliberately are strictly safer and need no new dependency. **Why no new admin UI:** the Ops alerts page already shows the relevant criticals. **Alternative considered:** an executable `scripts/reconcile-payments.mjs` (rejected — Ponytail-heavier, prod-connection risk; documented queries cover the same checks). Pinned by `tests/docs/payment-reconciliation-readiness.test.ts` (live-disabled language; forbidden actions + rollback present; reconciliation snippets are read-only — no `INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/DROP/ALTER`; #281 authoritative-success + webhook-backstop + ops-alert-visibility documented; Stripe gate expectations: one `paymentIntents.create`, one `refunds.create`, zero `charges.create`, zero `checkout.sessions`; runbook says enablement is a separate explicit owner-approved step). The existing `tests/docs/live-payments-readiness.test.ts` pins remain green (all edits additive). **Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement has not started — it is still a separate future PR.**

### Payment success persistence is authoritative (PR #281, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-29):** close the deep-review live-payment blocker that `runSessionPaymentCharge` could return a normal `ok:true, outcome:"succeeded"` after Stripe succeeded **even if Hone failed to persist the success on the local `payment_charge_attempts` ledger** (DB error) or the conditional UPDATE affected **zero rows** — a real-money / unstamped-ledger split reported to the practitioner as fully done. **The rule is now authoritative: a NORMAL `succeeded` result requires Stripe success AND a proven local-ledger write.** **Ponytail pass: no migration, no new dependency, no new public status, no UI work, no live-payment change — make the existing charge result safer inside one file.** Reuses the existing `needs_manual_review` outcome (already surfaced by both server actions — `app/(app)/clients/[id]/sessions/[sessionId]/payment-actions.ts` and `app/(app)/calendar/[id]/manual-fee-actions.ts`), the existing `recordOpsAlert` helper, the PR #263 zero-row `.select("id")` detection, and the PR #179 webhook reconciliation as the eventual-consistency backstop — and **mirrors the refund helper** (`lib/billing/payment-refund.ts`), which already returns `needs_manual_review` on `writeOkErr || zeroRows`. **Change (one runtime file, `lib/billing/session-payment-charge.ts`):** `writeSucceededOutcome` goes from `Promise<void>` to `Promise<SuccessPersistenceResult>` = `{persisted:true} | {persisted:false; reason:"db_error"|"zero_rows"}`; the **DB-error** branch now raises a **critical** ops alert `session_payment_succeeded_write_failed` (PR #263 only logged it to stderr) and returns `{persisted:false, reason:"db_error"}`; the **zero-row** branch keeps its critical `session_payment_succeeded_write_zero_rows` alert and returns `{persisted:false, reason:"zero_rows"}`; a proven single-row write returns `{persisted:true}`. **Both** success callers — the create/confirm path and `reconcileExistingPaymentIntent` — branch: `persisted:true` → the existing succeeded result; `persisted:false` → `ok:false, outcome:"needs_manual_review"` with a safe operator message + non-sensitive reconciliation ids (`stripePaymentIntentId`, `attemptId`) and **no retry** (the deterministic idempotency key + Stripe 24h replay already prevent a double charge). **No migration:** manual-review is a return value, not a DB status; the row stays `pending_stripe` for the webhook backstop. **Preserved unchanged:** claim-before-charge, idempotency key, live-mode hard block, exactly one `paymentIntents.create` / one `refunds.create`, webhook reconciliation, the refund helper, PaymentIntent metadata, Stripe gates. No card data / raw Stripe payload / secrets / sensitive client data in any new log, alert, or result. **Alternative considered:** a dedicated new `success_unpersisted` public status / a ledger `needs_review` column (rejected — the existing `needs_manual_review` outcome already models indeterminate, and a new column needs a migration for no behavioral gain). Pinned by `tests/lib/billing/payment-success-persistence.test.ts` and the new DB-error critical-alert assertion in `tests/lib/billing/payment-outcome-zero-row.test.ts`. **Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement has not started.** **Deferred (still):** the remaining live-payment readiness items in docs/16 — this PR does not start controlled live-payment enablement.

### Chloe record-keeping feedback — disinfectants + exposure incidents (PR #280, migration 0096)

**Decision (2026-06-29):** address Chloe's Record Keeping feedback, **record-keeping only** (no charting). Three workflows: **(1) Disinfectant discard/replace-by reminder** — add `record_keeping_disinfectants.discard_due_date` (nullable), distinct from `date_prepared` and the actual `date_discarded`; surface a **read-time** due/overdue/due-soon badge on the Records page (computed in studio tz). **(2) Operator dropdown** — same-studio staff (current user + active practitioners) with a free-text "Other" fallback, populating the pre-existing `operator_practitioner_id` + `operator_name`; the server resolves the name via a studio-scoped lookup (cross-studio id can't attach); edit now writes the FK too. **(3) Exposure-incident person selector** — Client / Staff-or-self / Other, autofilling the existing free-text name/phone/address fields from same-studio clients/staff (editable), no FK stored.

**Why Option A (read-time alert) over Option B (cron bell):** smallest safe surface — no new cron route, no external scheduler, no notification-spam/idempotency complexity, no email/SMS — and fully testable locally/CI, while still giving Chloe a visible due/overdue reminder in Record Keeping. **Proactive bell + email/SMS are deferred** (documented in docs/08); they would reuse the existing `practitioner_notifications` table + a daily cron + a `reminder_sent_at` idempotency marker. **Why no schema change for operator/exposure:** `operator_practitioner_id` already existed (0085); exposure autofill only pre-fills existing free-text columns (no client/practitioner FK → owner-only read posture from 0088 unchanged, and we avoid copying client data / cross-studio RLS complexity). **Migration 0096** is additive/backward-compatible but the app **writes** the column, so apply-0096-then-merge (like 0095). **No charting / Treatment Photos / payment / booking / intake / RLS change.** Pinned by tests/lib/record-keeping/disinfectant-status.test.ts, tests/app/records/record-keeping-feedback.test.ts, tests/migrations/0096-disinfectant-discard-due-date.test.ts, tests/db/record-keeping-discard-due.db.test.ts.

### Chloe real-charting feedback — charting UX + bug fixes (PR #279, migration 0095)

**Decision (2026-06-28):** address Chloe's same-day feedback after she charted real treatments, **charting only** (record-keeping feedback — disinfectant discard reminder, operator dropdown, exposure-incident person selector — is **deferred to PR #280**). Smallest-safe per item: **(1) Numbing** — no field existed, so add `session_blocks.numbing_status` (text; NULL = Not recorded; CHECK `none`/`used`) + a 3-choice control; factual record only (no advice/dosing). **(2) Body-map underarms** — the map is zone-only, so picking a sub-area (Underarms) flooded the whole **Arms** shape; now the broad zone shows only a subtle "contains the selected area" tint, exact area via the chip + "Area being charted" line (no map redraw). **(3) Probe lot** — suggest the latest current probe lot from `record_keeping_sterile_items` (read-only) and require an explicit **Confirm** (`session_blocks.probe_lot_confirmed`, default false); typing un-confirms; manual entry preserved; never auto-confirmed. **(4) Energy** — moved under Treatment readings (UI only; same `energy_level`). **(5) OmniBlend** — galvanic before thermolysis, no thermolysis duration, no galvanic intensity; keyed on `apilus_modality === "Omniblend"` only (other modalities unchanged pending Chloe; hidden inputs never zero historical values). **(6) Tolerance** — label-based control (Comfortable … Needed pause/stopped early) over the **unchanged** 1-5 `tolerance_rating` storage (old numeric maps cleanly). **(7) Observation chips** — were append-only (couldn't unselect); now toggles that add/remove a token in the notes text (manual text preserved); reaction chips stay single-select with "No visible reaction" mutually exclusive.

**Why this shape:** keep the smallest schema change (one additive migration, two columns) and prefer labels/UI/data-mapping over data changes (tolerance keeps its numeric column; energy is a pure UI move). **Migration 0095** is additive + backward-compatible, but because the app **writes** the new columns, it must be applied to prod **before** the #279 deploy serves traffic — recommended sequence is apply-0095-then-merge (unlike 0094, which was forward/backward compatible and applied after merge). **No payment / live-mode / Treatment Photos / booking / intake / RLS change.** **Alternative considered:** shoehorn numbing into free-text notes (rejected — not queryable, not a clean record) and a fully redrawn body map (rejected — out of scope). Pinned by tests/lib/sessions/clinical-response.test.ts, tests/lib/comments.test.ts, tests/app/sessions/charting-feedback.test.ts, tests/app/sessions/body-map-area-picker.test.ts, tests/migrations/0095-charting-numbing-probe-lot.test.ts, tests/db/charting-numbing-probe-lot.db.test.ts.

### Tenant consistency constraints for sensitive child tables (PR #278, migration 0094)

**Decision (2026-06-28):** the deep review's remaining P1 — several sensitive child tables enforced `studio_id` via RLS but did not prove their PARENT rows belong to the same studio (at the DB layer a row could carry studio_id=A while pointing at a client/session/appointment/import-batch from studio B). UUID parent ids are unguessable and RLS scopes reads, so the practical surface is small, but multi-tenant defense-in-depth wants it in the schema. **DB hardening only — no UI/product/payment/RLS/app behavior change.** The payment subsystem (`appointment_payments`, `payment_charge_attempts`) and `treatment_images` (0093 trigger) already enforced same-studio parents; this extends the **composite-FK** pattern to the remaining member-writable clinical/import tables.

**Migration 0094** (idempotent; additive; non-destructive): adds parent unique keys `sessions(studio_id,id)`, `session_blocks(session_id,id)`, `import_batches(studio_id,id)` (clients/appointments already had `(id,studio_id)`), then **composite same-studio FKs**: `sessions`→clients`(studio_id,client_id)` (cascade) + appointments`(studio_id,appointment_id)` (set null), `session_blocks`→sessions`(studio_id,session_id)` (cascade), `client_intake_forms`/`treatment_plans`→clients`(studio_id,client_id)` (cascade), `imported_treatment_memories`→clients`(studio_id,client_id)` (cascade) + import_batches`(studio_id,import_batch_id)` (restrict), and `electrolysis_entries`→session_blocks`(session_id,block_id)` (set null — the entry's block must belong to its own session; this table has no `studio_id`, tenancy flows via the session). **Composite FKs, NOT triggers** — deliberately avoiding the PR #276 immediate-CHECK-vs-SET-NULL-cascade pitfall; on **PG17** the column-list `ON DELETE SET NULL (col)` nulls only the parent id, never the NOT-NULL `studio_id`. Each composite FK **REPLACES** the prior single-column FK (mirroring its ON DELETE; behavior-preserving) so each table pair keeps exactly **one** relationship — **two FKs between a table pair make PostgREST embedded selects ambiguous** ("Could not embed because more than one relationship was found"), which broke app reads like `sessions.select("… session_blocks(…)")` when the first cut added composites *beside* the singles. The composite joins on the same key (studio_id is always consistent), so embeds return identical rows. `electrolysis_entries_session_id_fkey` is **kept** (different pair). `MATCH SIMPLE` lets a NULL parent id skip the check (detach-to-NULL stays legal); delete/cascade semantics unchanged. **No RLS policy changed.** **App change (minimal):** because the single FKs were dropped, **3 PostgREST embed hints** that used `alias:<fk_column>(…)` (resolving via the old single FK) were updated to the bare table name so they resolve via the composite FK — `lib/billing/session-payment-eligibility.ts` (`appointments:appointment_id` → `appointments`), `lib/record-keeping/queries.ts` (`clients:client_id` → `clients`), and `app/(app)/global-search-actions.ts` (the session_blocks→sessions→clients nested embed). No response-shape change; `appointments→clients` was unchanged so its `client:client_id(name)` embed stays valid. Pinned by tests/app/tenant-consistency-embeds.test.ts. The DB FKs still enforce tenancy for every write path incl. service-role.

**Production preflight (read-only, 2026-06-28): 0 existing cross-studio rows** across all eight checks; row counts tiny — the composite FKs validate without rewriting data; embedded in the migration header. **Already-protected/deferred:** payment tables + `treatment_images` (untouched); `laser_entries` (single parent, no mismatch possible); `photos` (legacy unwired); `ops_alerts`/audit/notifications + portal/stripe-provisioning tables (service-role-written, not member-writable — theoretical, deferred). **Pinned by** tests/db/tenant-consistency.db.test.ts (cross-studio insert + update rejected per table; same-studio valid; client-delete cascade; appointment/block detach-to-NULL; electrolysis block-from-another-session rejected; RLS cross-studio read still blocked) + tests/migrations/0094-tenant-consistency-constraints.test.ts (SQL pin; no RLS/payment/trigger change); PR #271–#277 tests still pass. **Migration-first: 0094 must NOT be applied to production until explicitly approved after merge.** No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement not started.

### Treatment image content validation + EXIF stripping (PR #277, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-28):** the last item from the PR #276 deep review — uploads still trusted the browser-declared MIME + size. **Upload hardening only — no new product feature, no UI change, no payment change, no public exposure, no annotation/OCR/AI/comparison, no migration (app-layer only; the 0093 schema/constraints/triggers are untouched).** A new **server-only sharp module** `lib/images/treatment-image-sanitize.ts` (`sanitizeTreatmentImage`, Node runtime) now: decodes the actual bytes (never `file.type`); rejects anything not a genuine JPEG/PNG/WebP — SVG, HEIC/HEIF, PDF, HTML, video, corrupt/garbage, **fake-MIME** (detected ≠ declared), empty, and decompression bombs (`limitInputPixels ≈ 100 MP`); and **re-encodes the same format WITHOUT metadata** (`.rotate()` auto-orients then drops the tag; sharp drops EXIF/GPS/XMP/ICC on encode unless explicitly preserved, which it never is). `uploadTreatmentImageAction` reads the bytes → sanitizes → uploads the **sanitized bytes** with the **sanitized content type**, builds the path from the sanitized extension, and stores the **sanitized content type + sanitized size**; original filename remains display-only; invalid uploads return a generic `"Upload a valid JPEG, PNG, or WebP image."` and **never reach storage** (no ops alert on normal user mistakes). PR #276 path validator / signer / parent-consistency / identity-immutability / orphan-cleanup all intact.

**Dependency:** `sharp@^0.34.5` promoted from transitive (Next 15's image optimizer) to a **direct** dependency — it was already installed and Vercel-built (Next uses it in prod), so no native-build/optional-dep risk; the lockfile change is just the promotion (version unchanged). **Decompression-bomb guard** caps input pixels; output preserves input format (jpeg→jpeg, png→png, webp→webp). **Deferred:** AV/virus scanning, perceptual hashing, thumbnails, session/block attach-at-upload. **Pinned by** tests/lib/images/treatment-image-sanitize.test.ts (real-format accept; fake/SVG/HEIC/PDF/HTML/corrupt/empty/mismatch reject; EXIF stripped; deterministic output) + tests/app/clients/treatment-image-content-validation.test.ts (upload uses sanitized bytes/type/size, invalid → no storage upload, orphan-cleanup preserved, no getPublicUrl/public exposure); PR #271–#276 image tests still pass. No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement not started.

### Treatment Photos storage boundary hardening (PR #276, migration 0093)

**Decision (2026-06-27):** a deep security review of the live PR #271–#274 treatment-image feature found P1 trust-boundary gaps: (1) `storage.objects` had **authenticated** select/insert policies for `treatment-images`, so a member could read/write objects directly (bypassing the server actions); (2) the metadata insert RLS only checked `is_studio_member(studio_id)`, so a member could insert a **forged row** (arbitrary `storage_path`/`storage_bucket`, cross-studio `client_id`) and the service-role signer would then **sign that forged path** (cross-studio exfiltration); (3) path-bearing identity columns were mutable; (4) parent rows (client/session/block) weren't enforced same-studio. **Hardening only — no new product feature, no UI change, no payment/live-mode change, no public/client exposure, no annotation/OCR/AI/comparison.**

**Migration 0093** (additive, idempotent; storage statements exception-wrapped like 0092): keeps the bucket private; **drops the authenticated `storage.objects` policies** for `treatment-images` (objects become **service-role only** — the app never used direct member object access, so this only removes the bypass); adds CHECK constraints `storage_bucket='treatment-images'` and `storage_path ~ '^<studio_id>/<client_id>/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+\.(jpg|jpeg|png|webp)$'` (bound to the row's own ids; closes wrong-bucket / wrong-prefix / traversal / multi-segment) + "block requires session"; and a BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger `enforce_treatment_image_integrity` that enforces client∈studio / session∈studio+client / block∈session+studio and **freezes the identity columns** post-insert (archive only flips `deleted_at`/`deleted_by`). **App:** a central pure `validateTreatmentImagePath` (`lib/images/treatment-images.ts`) is wired into BOTH `getTreatmentImageSignedUrlAction` and the page pre-signer — a forged/malformed/cross-studio/cross-client path is **rejected, never signed** (critical `treatment_image_sign_rejected_invalid_path` ops alert); the upload now raises a **critical** `treatment_image_orphan_cleanup_failed` alert when post-insert-failure object cleanup fails. Path is still server-constructed; MIME+size validation unchanged; **no public URLs / no `getPublicUrl`**; signed URLs short-TTL + never stored.

**Deferred:** image-content validation / EXIF stripping / magic-byte sniffing (needs a dependency — next PR), and the session/block attach-at-upload write-path (0093 makes it *safe* when it lands). **Migration-first: production migration 0093 must NOT be applied until explicitly approved after merge** (production has no treatment_images rows yet, so the new CHECK/trigger cannot reject existing rows; a preflight query is embedded in the migration). **Pinned by** tests/lib/images/treatment-images.test.ts (path validator) + tests/migrations/0093-harden-treatment-image-storage.test.ts (SQL pin) + tests/db/treatment-image-hardening.db.test.ts (db lane) + tests/app/clients/treatment-image-hardening.test.ts (wiring); PR #271/#272/#273/#274 image tests still pass. No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement not started.

**Tags:** a scope badge — **"Client photo"** (floor, every image has a client) → **"Session photo"** (session_id) → **"Block photo"** (session_block_id, most specific wins); an area badge when a block is attached — **"Treatment area: <area · side · detail>"** (reusing the canonical `sessionBlockSideLabel` + the `session-blocks-view.tsx` format) or **"Area not recorded"**; plus the existing **"Uploaded <date>"** and a muted **"Clinical reference"** label. **Current uploads are client-only** (`uploadTreatmentImageAction` writes `session_id`/`session_block_id` as null), so today every photo shows "Client photo"; the session/block/area badges light up automatically once a future write-path attaches photos to a session/block — **that attach-at-upload write path is deferred** (no schema added speculatively). **No raw UUIDs / storage paths / bucket names / signed-URL text are rendered.** No annotation/drawing/OCR/AI, no side-by-side comparison, no consent/export/marketing, no public/client exposure. **Pinned by** tests/app/clients/treatment-photo-context.test.ts (pure scope/area-label units + UI wiring: labels computed server-side, ContextTags on cards + modal, no raw IDs/paths in the client); PR #271/#272/#273 tests still pass. No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Secure practitioner-only image storage V1 (PR #271, migration 0092)

**Decision (2026-06-27):** add secure, practitioner-only image storage so treatment memory can include visual reference material. **Storage + access only — no annotation, no drawing/sketch, no OCR/AI, no public/client exposure, no full chart-template builder** (all deferred). A **private** Supabase Storage bucket `treatment-images` (`public = false`) + a `treatment_images` metadata table (studio_id + client_id required; session_id/session_block_id optional; storage_bucket/path, sanitized original_filename, content_type, size_bytes, uploaded_by, created_at/updated_at, soft-delete deleted_at/deleted_by). **No public URLs**: viewing mints a short-TTL signed URL via a server action; the practitioner UI lives at `/clients/<id>/images`.

**Access model (two-plane, defense-in-depth):** metadata reads/writes go through the **RLS client** (studio-scoped `is_studio_member` policies: member select/insert/update, **no delete**; `revoke truncate, delete`). The **storage plane** (upload + `createSignedUrl`) goes through the **service-role** client ONLY AFTER the server action (a) resolves studio from the authenticated session and (b) re-checks the target client/row belongs to that studio; the studio.id is never taken from input, the storage path is server-generated (`<studio_id>/<client_id>/<uuid>.<ext>`), and a client-supplied path is never signed. Bucket is private so service-role power + the app gate are the authority; storage.objects policies (scoped by the first path segment) are belt-and-braces and are wrapped so a platform-permission edge cannot fail the table migration. Upload validation: MIME allowlist (jpeg/png/webp), 15 MB cap, reject SVG/PDF, sanitize filename. **Not reused:** the legacy unwired `public.photos` table (0001) — it carries before/after/progress semantics the product avoids and none of the security metadata; left untouched.

**Migration-first:** migration 0092 adds the bucket + table + RLS + storage policies. **Update: the production migration + bucket were applied after merge and verified** — the `treatment_images` table exists and the private `treatment-images` bucket exists with `public=false` (the storage statements applied cleanly; no manual dashboard fallback was needed). **Deferred:** annotation/drawing, OCR/AI, client/portal exposure, hard delete of objects, session/block-level attach UI, bulk upload. **Pinned by** tests/lib/images/treatment-images.test.ts (validator/path), tests/migrations/0092-treatment-images.test.ts (SQL pin: private bucket, RLS, no-delete), tests/db/treatment-images.db.test.ts (RLS cross-studio isolation, soft-delete, db lane), tests/app/clients/treatment-images.test.ts (signed-URL-only, service-role gated, no public exposure / OCR / Jane). No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).
