### Visual treatment-area picker (PR #269, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-27):** make charting area selection feel like a **visual chart part** rather than a plain field. PR #268 added area labels/context but not the visual picker Chloe expected. V1 frames the existing region-grouped `AreaPicker` (chips over `AREA_REGIONS`) inside a **"Chart part" → "Treatment area" card** on the session-block charting form, adds the helper "Choose the area for this chart entry," and shows a **live "Area being charted: …" preview** (area + side + specifics) with an **"Area not recorded"** fallback. **Deliberately a simple visual grouped picker, NOT an anatomical image/body-map** — that, plus image storage/upload, photo annotation, sketch/drawing canvas, OCR/AI, and a full chart-template builder, are explicitly deferred.

**Reused, not built:** the existing `AreaPicker` + `AREA_REGIONS` catalog and the structured `session_blocks.primary_area / side / custom_area_detail` (migration 0039) — **no migration, no new field, no new dependency, no new component** (framing/wording added in the charting form only; the shared `AreaPicker` is unchanged so the treatment-plan editor is unaffected). Save behavior and legacy compatibility are unchanged (area stays optional). **Jane** chart-part/body-chart references were product-category inspiration only — no Jane UI/wording/assets/thumbnails/URLs copied (the existing source-grep test still bans any Jane CDN/thumbnail asset URL in app/components/docs; a new test pins no upload/canvas/OCR primitive in the picker/form). **Deferred:** anatomical body-map/diagram, image storage, image/photo upload, drawing/sketch annotation, OCR/AI, full template builder, and the #215–#265 tracker backfill. **Pinned by** tests/app/sessions/visual-area-picker.test.ts (grouped chips + chart-part card + "Area being charted" preview + no upload/canvas/OCR/Jane) and the PR #268 chart-parts tests (still green). No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Chart parts: treatment area visible across charting + memory (PR #268, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-27):** introduce lightweight "chart parts" framing whose first part is the **treatment area / area being charted**, and make that body-area context visible in charting, saved entries, and treatment memory. **Charting context only — not image storage, not a body-map/drawing, not sketch, not photo/file upload, not OCR/AI, not medical advice.** Body-area context matters because treatment memory is Hone's moat: the practitioner needs to know what was done on a specific area last time. V1 is a display/labeling change that REUSES existing fields — `session_blocks.primary_area / side / custom_area_detail` (migration 0039, already captured area-first by the `AreaPicker` over the `AREA_REGIONS` catalog + "Other") and `ImportedTreatmentMemory.treatment_area_text` (0089) and `treatment_plans.treatment_areas` — so **no migration, no new field, no new dependency**.

**Reused, not built:** the charting form and `session-blocks-view` already capture/show the structured area; V1 adds (1) a "Recorded area" / "Area not recorded" eyebrow on each saved entry, (2) a labeled "Treatment area" line + an "Area not recorded" fallback + a "Latest recorded setup — <area>" line on the Before Today / treatment-memory card (the setup is tied to its area via the existing `TreatmentIntelligence.areas[0].name`), and (3) an explicit "Imported area" label on imported memory (kept in its separate amber, provenance-noted section). Treatment Intelligence already aggregates per-area and is left as-is (re-keying its grouping would re-bucket historical area cards). **Jane** (Jane's "Chart Parts" guide) was used ONLY as product-category inspiration — no Jane UI, wording, screenshots, thumbnails, assets, or URLs are copied (a source-grep test bans any Jane CDN/thumbnail asset URL in app/components/docs). **Deferred:** image storage, body-map/drawing, sketch, file/photo upload, OCR/AI, a full chart-template builder, the dashboard-roster area line, records relabel, and the #215–#265 tracker backfill. **Pinned by** tests/app/clients/chart-parts-area.test.ts (labels + no-Jane-asset) and the extended tests/app/clients/before-today.test.ts (setup tied to area). No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Intake modality-specific review flags (PR #267, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-26):** extend the PR #266 intake review flags so each chart-mapped flag also carries **modality/category badges** from Chloe's clinic contraindication reference chart — **Review before thermolysis**, **Review before continuous/galvanic current**, **Medical authorization may be required**, **Precaution noted** (and a reserved "Authorization depends on practitioner review" for "depends on the case" rows). **Hard line unchanged: Hone does not make medical decisions — it surfaces "this intake answer maps to modality-specific review," and it does not approve or deny treatment.** Practitioner workflow support only; not medical advice; not automated treatment approval/denial. Badges are still DERIVED at read time (no migration, no new field, no persisted flag/risk table, no free-text parsing, no OCR/AI, no image storage, no booking/charting block); the uploaded chart image is NOT parsed dynamically — its rows are encoded once as a static `CHART_MODALITIES` lookup keyed by the existing flag id.

**Reused, not built:** extended `lib/intake/review-flags.ts` in place — V1 `level`/`wording`/sort are unchanged (V1 tests pass), a `badges: IntakeModality[]` field is added per flag, and the practitioner card renders the badges (it previously rendered the single level pill). Only rows backed by an EXISTING structured intake answer are mapped — heart/pacemaker, pregnancy, controlled epilepsy, cancer, hepatitis+HIV (blood-borne), diabetes, and blood-thinners→prolonged-bleeding. **Diabetes** has one generic intake answer (no T1/T2/gestational subtype), so it surfaces the conservative UNION of all three diabetes chart rows. **Chart rows with NO structured field are not invented** (puberty, haemophilia, multiple sclerosis / nervous-system disorder, radiotherapy, chemotherapy, arteritis, retinal implant, circulatory problems, hyper/hypotension, infectious disease, total paralysis); flags with no chart row keep the V1 generic fallback badge. **Why no migration:** badges derive from already-structured answers. **Wording:** allowed chart phrases only; The avoided clinical-decision wording is never rendered ("contraindication reference chart" appears only as product context in docs); forbidden clinical-decision wording is banned from the module + card by a scoped source-grep test. **Deferred:** body-area charting, secure image storage, OCR/AI, a client-overview indicator, the #215–#265 tracker backfill. **Pinned by** tests/lib/intake/review-flags-modality.test.ts (chart mapping, unmapped-not-invented, no free-text parsing, badge wording) + the extended tests/app/clients/intake-review-flags.test.ts (badges render practitioner-only, not on the public route). No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Intake review flags (PR #266, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-26):** surface practitioner-only "intake review needed" flags on the intake review page when existing intake answers indicate items Chloe wants reviewed before treatment. **Hard line: Hone does not make clinical decisions — it surfaces intake answers for practitioner review only.** Product/workflow hardening only — no migration, no new field, no free-text parsing, no AI/OCR, no persisted risk score, no booking/charting block, no treatment approval/denial logic; practitioner-only, no public/client-facing exposure. Flags are DERIVED at read time by a pure rule table (`lib/intake/review-flags.ts`) over the structured answers already in `client_intake_forms.responses` (multi_select option values, single_select values, yes_no = "yes"). Each flag cites its source ("Based on intake response: <question> — <answer>"), carries allowed wording only — "Medical authorization may be required" / "Review before treatment" / "Precaution noted" — and the card closes with "Use professional judgment and clinic policy."

**Reused, not built:** the existing `client_intake_forms.responses` JSONB + the `lib/intake/questions.ts` keys; the practitioner intake review page (`/clients/<id>/intake`, server component gated by `requirePractitionerWithStudio()` + studio RLS) and its existing derived-summary card idiom (EpiPen banner / `AllergiesSummary` / `FitzpatrickSummary`, each "renders nothing when empty"). Allergy / EpiPen signals already have dedicated cards and are NOT duplicated as flags. **Why no migration:** flags derive from already-structured answers; no schema, no risk table. **Forbidden clinical-decision wording is banned** from the new module + card; a scoped source-grep test enforces it, and the banned terms appear only inside that test's negative assertions. **Categories that cannot be mapped** (specific non-listed prescriptions, moles/lesions, exact allergen identity — only free text) are deferred, not invented; the practitioner still reads the raw answer in the per-question grid. **Deferred:** a compact "Before Today" client-overview indicator (V2), image storage, body-area charting, AI/OCR, the #215–#265 tracker backfill. **Pinned by** tests/lib/intake/review-flags.test.ts (pure deriver + wording safety) and tests/app/clients/intake-review-flags.test.ts (pure/no-I/O, allowed wording present, forbidden wording absent, practitioner-only / not on the public `/intake/[token]` route). No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Reminder cron status signal (PR #265, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-26):** make the EXTERNAL appointment-reminder scheduler operator-visible. `/api/cron/appointment-reminders` is driven by an external every-15-minute scheduler (cron-job.org, `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET`) because Vercel's plan caps cron at once-per-day (PR #258); if that scheduler is disabled/misconfigured/failing, reminders silently stop and nothing is recorded — the existing `cron_route_failed`/`reminder_send_exhausted` ops_alerts only fire when the route RUNS and throws/exhausts, so a stopped scheduler produces silence. This PR adds the smallest positive heartbeat so the admin console can show healthy/stale/missing. **Operational reliability hardening only — no new scheduler, queue, retry, dependency, migration, or reminder-send change; no customer-facing UI.** On each authorized successful run the route writes a single Upstash KV key `reminder_cron:last_success` (ISO timestamp + non-sensitive aggregate run counts; best-effort/fail-open; 24h TTL); the operator-only `/admin` console reads it and a pure classifier (`computeReminderSchedulerStatus`) marks **healthy** (last success ≤ 45 min), **stale** (older), or **missing** (none). The 45-min threshold = 3 missed `*/15` cycles, derived from the shared `CRON_INTERVAL_MINUTES` so it cannot drift.

**Reused, not built:** the Upstash Redis already required in production (PR #262) — a single overwritten KV key, NOT the append-only `ops_alerts` table (which would gain ~96 info rows/day and pollute the failure dashboard; `ops_alerts` keeps carrying the FAILURE direction via `cron_route_failed`/`reminder_send_exhausted`). The admin status card mirrors the existing console card/Flag idiom and reads via the service-role-gated `/admin` page. **Why no migration:** a single KV heartbeat needs no schema, ships with the app deploy (no separate prod-migration step), and reuses required infra. **Alternative considered:** a `cron_run_status` table (Option C) — rejected as needless schema + a separate prod migration for a lightweight signal; deriving last-success from `reminder_*_sent_at` — rejected (a quiet run with nothing due stamps nothing, so it cannot distinguish "didn't run" from "nothing due"). **Logging/security:** the heartbeat stores a timestamp + counts only — never CRON_SECRET, email, phone, name, notes, token, or URL; the auth gate + 401 path are unchanged and the heartbeat is recorded only AFTER the gate (an unauthorized request records nothing). **Pinned by** tests/lib/cron/reminder-heartbeat.test.ts (pure healthy/stale/missing classifier) and tests/app/cron/reminder-heartbeat-wiring.test.ts (heartbeat after the auth gate; non-sensitive payload; fail-open; admin surfacing). No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Raw appointment cancellation_token column removed (PR #264, migration 0091)

**Decision (2026-06-26):** drop the legacy raw `appointments.cancellation_token` column so no raw appointment cancel/reschedule/manage token is retained at rest — closing the deploy-window compatibility that PR #260 (migration 0090) deliberately left open. PR #260 hashed appointment tokens at rest (`cancellation_token_hash`), backfilled every row's hash, added a `BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE` trigger that auto-hashed a raw token when the hash was absent, and made the two token-verifying RPCs DUAL-MATCH (`cancellation_token_hash = $x OR cancellation_token = $x`) so in-flight OLD-app requests passing a raw token kept working. That window is now closed. **Token/storage-hardening only — no new token system, no dependency, no UI, no behavior change to cancel/reschedule/manage links, no payment/live-mode change.**

**Migration 0091** (internal order is deliberate — plpgsql bodies are not dependency-checked, so the raw references are removed BEFORE the column drop): (1) re-creates `public_cancel_appointment_with_token` (5-arg) HASH-ONLY (drops the `OR cancellation_token = p_token` branch); (2) re-creates `reschedule_appointment` HASH-ONLY (drops the dual-match raw branch AND the raw-column INSERT/shape-routing — the new token is stored only as `cancellation_token_hash`); (3) drops the dead 2-arg `public_cancel_appointment_with_token(text, text)` (migration 0033) which still referenced the raw column; (4) drops the deploy-window hashing trigger + its function; (5) drops the raw column (also removing the 0025 unique constraint + partial index). The canonical `cancellation_token_hash` column, its CHECK, and its partial unique are untouched.

**App:** the three link-rebuild surfaces that read `?? appt.cancellation_token` (internal-booking confirmation `app/(app)/calendar/actions.ts`; reminder email + SMS manage `app/api/cron/appointment-reminders/route.ts`) now always mint the stateless HMAC token (`generateCancellationToken`); the public cancel/reschedule/manage actions already looked up `cancellation_token_hash` only. The raw field is removed from the `Appointment` type (`lib/types/database.ts`). No raw token is read, written, or logged anywhere.

**Why safe / valid links still resolve:** every resolver hashes the incoming URL token (`hashAppointmentToken`) and matches `cancellation_token_hash`; 0090 backfilled that hash for every row that had a raw token, so already-emitted `/cancel/<raw>` links still resolve WITHOUT the raw column. New rows are hash-only; the only both-NULL rows are inert legacy non-confirmed rows no valid link reaches. The app reads the column only off `select("*")` and immediately falls back to HMAC, so the running app tolerates the drop; the re-created RPCs stay hash-compatible with the running app (it already passes hashes). **Migration-first:** **Update: migration 0091 is now applied + verified in production** — the raw `appointments.cancellation_token` column is gone (local == remote, none pending). It was run later under explicit approval, not as part of this PR. **Alternative considered:** null-but-keep + a guard (Option B) or code-first/drop-later (Option C) — rejected because the app is drop-tolerant and the in-migration RPC swap keeps the running app compatible, so the extra round-trip buys nothing. **Pinned by** tests/migrations/0091-drop-raw-cancellation-token.test.ts (migration shape), tests/db/appointment-token-hash.db.test.ts (hash-only storage, raw column absent → 42703, RPCs reject a raw token, hash lookup still cancels/reschedules), and tests/app/book/no-raw-appointment-token.test.ts (no app read/write of the raw column; type field removed). No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create remains. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Payment outcome zero-row detection (PR #263, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-26):** every payment-OUTCOME write must PROVE it affected a row; a zero-row update is now an explicit failure / manual-review condition, never silent success. Review flagged that the status-conditional outcome updates only checked the DB `error`: `.update({status:'succeeded'|'failed', …}).eq('id', x).eq('status','pending_stripe')` (and the webhook equivalents) return `error: null` with ZERO affected rows when the row left the guarded state between the read and the write (a concurrent action-layer/webhook race), so a real Stripe outcome could be recorded nowhere while the code continued as if it had been recorded. **App-layer hardening only — no migration, no new dependency, no new Stripe call, no live-payment change, no payment-amount/flow change.** Each of the **eight** outcome writes now appends `.select("id")` and branches on an empty result: the charge executor (`writeSucceededOutcome` → critical `session_payment_succeeded_write_zero_rows`; `writeFailedOutcome` → warning `session_payment_failed_write_zero_rows`), the refund helper (success + terminal-failure writes fold zero-row into their existing critical `payment_refund_*_write_failed` → `needs_manual_review`), and the four webhook reconcilers (`payment-webhook-reconciliation.ts` → a per-event `*_zero_rows` warning ops_alert + a `zeroRowNoMutation` return instead of falsely claiming `reconciledFromStatus`/`reconciledFromPending`/`outOfBandReconciled`). The out-of-band-refund handler's "reconciled" alert, previously fired unconditionally, is now gated behind rows>0.

**Reused, not built:** the exact `.update(...).eq(...).select("id")` + `if (!rows || rows.length === 0)` row-affected pattern already proven by the refund/receipt CLAIM sites (`payment-refund.ts` claim, `payment-receipt.ts` claim), plus the existing `recordOpsAlert` (`@/lib/ops/alerts`, already imported in all three files; redacts secrets, never throws) + `logInternal` channels. **No state machine, no new module. Why no migration:** the affected-row proof is a read-shape change (PostgREST returns the updated rows whenever `.select()` is chained); no column/constraint/RPC change. **Alternative considered:** a runtime fail-CLOSED on the mutation when zero rows — rejected, because the usual zero-row is a benign concurrent reconcile reaching the same end-state, so the safe response is a structured alert + manual-review surfacing (which `recordOpsAlert` provides) rather than blocking the flow / inventing state.

**Logging/alerting:** zero-row alerts carry only safe fields — `attempt_id`, the attempted/`read` status enum, `stripe_charge_id`/`stripePaymentIntentId` where present — never a raw Stripe payload, `last_payment_error`, card data, secret, token, or customer email/phone (and `recordOpsAlert` redacts defensively). **Scope / not done (deferred):** receipt-delivery status writes (`payment-receipt.ts` sent/failed/release legs — not a money outcome; the failed/release legs already alert) and the two idempotent id-backfills (`stripe_charge_id`/`stripe_refund_id` on already-terminal rows — 0-or-1 by design) are intentionally left. **Pinned by** tests/lib/billing/payment-outcome-zero-row.test.ts (each outcome write proves a row via `.select("id")`; each has a zero-row alert / `zeroRowNoMutation` / `needs_manual_review` branch; no raw payload/PII/secret in the alert payloads; exactly one paymentIntents.create + one refunds.create; no live-mode flip). No Stripe gate / live-mode change; exactly one paymentIntents.create remains. Live payments remain disabled; controlled live-payment enablement not started.

### Rate-limit production env gate (PR #262, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-25):** fail the **production build/deploy** when the public rate-limit env vars (`UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL`, `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN`) are missing, so a misconfigured production deploy can no longer silently ship with public abuse protection disabled. Review flagged that `lib/rate-limit/public.ts` **fails open on missing config**: `getRedis()` returns `null` when either var is unset and every limiter returns `{ allowed: true }`, leaving the unauthenticated public surfaces (booking slots/submit, portal magic-link, cancel/reschedule/intake token routes, marketing waitlist/demo) with no abuse dampener and only a once-per-cold-start `ratelimit_disabled_env_missing` log line. **Build/CI safety-script change only — no new rate limiter, no dependency, no schema/migration, no route behavior change.** New `scripts/check-production-env-gates.mjs` (modeled on `scripts/check-stripe-gates.mjs`) is wired into the `build` script (`node scripts/check-production-env-gates.mjs && next build`), which is exactly what Vercel runs for a production deploy. It enforces ONLY when `VERCEL_ENV === "production"` (a real Vercel production build) and is a **no-op SKIP** for local/CI/preview builds and unit tests — deliberately NOT keyed on `NODE_ENV`, which `next build` sets to `production` everywhere and would wrongly fail local/CI builds that legitimately run without Upstash. It prints variable **names only, never values**.

**Missing config vs provider outage (deliberate split):** missing config in production = **fail the deploy** (this gate). A transient Upstash **outage** is a different thing, left to the existing runtime fail-open (`try/catch → { allowed: true }` with a throttled `ratelimit_backend_unavailable` alarm) — a limiter outage must never block a real booking. **No emergency bypass — by design:** a presence check does not depend on Upstash being reachable, so an outage never trips it (the vars stay set); the only thing a bypass flag could do is re-enable the exact silent prod fail-open this gate removes, and "temporary" prod fail-open flags rot into permanently-on. A test pins that no `*BYPASS*`/`*ALLOW*` env can disable the gate. **Alternative considered:** a runtime fail-CLOSED on mutation routes when config is missing in prod — rejected as a behavior change that risks blocking real bookings; the deploy gate prevents the missing-config state from shipping in the first place, so runtime stays untouched.

**Scope / not done (deferred):** runtime route behavior is unchanged (deploy-time guard, not fail-closed-on-mutation). The **`portal/verify` magic-link consume** action (`app/portal/verify/[token]/actions.ts`) is the one public mutation route with NO limiter at all — a coverage gap mitigated by its single-use atomic consume + high-entropy token; adding a limiter there is a separate route change, deferred. Other non-rate-limit env degradations on public surfaces (e.g. `RESEND_API_KEY` missing → emails silently don't send behind a generic success) are out of scope; the adversarial sweep confirmed the rate limiter is the ONLY abuse control that fails open (CRON/Twilio/webhook/signing/admin/Stripe/app-origin all already fail closed). **Pinned by** tests/scripts/check-production-env-gates.test.ts (production + each var missing → exit 1 naming the var; both present → exit 0 PASS; preview/unset → exit 0 SKIP; never prints env values; keys on `VERCEL_ENV` not `NODE_ENV`; no bypass env); docs/10 marks the Upstash vars required-in-production. No Stripe/payments/billing/booking-business-rule change. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Public booking PII log minimization (PR #261, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-25):** stop writing raw client PII into the **public, unauthenticated** booking server logs. Review flagged that `app/book/[slug]/actions.ts` logged the raw normalized booker email and an internal client UUID in two internal error paths — `public_booking_archived_client_collision` (logged `normalizedEmail` + `archivedClientId`) and `public_booking_unique_race_unresolved` (logged `normalizedEmail` + the raw Postgres `error.message`). The sink is `logInternalBookingError` → `console.error(JSON.stringify(detail))` (→ Vercel function logs) with **no redaction layer**, so whatever a call site puts in `detail` is emitted verbatim. **Logging-payload change only — no logging system, no dependency, no migration, no schema change, no booking behavior change.** Every `logInternalBookingError` payload in the public booking surface now carries safe fields only: the Postgres `code` (sqlstate — low-cardinality ops signal), `studioId` (tenant id, already logged), and — where a booker email is in scope — `emailFingerprint: hashFingerprint(normalizedEmail)`. The raw `normalizedEmail`, the `archivedClientId`, and every raw DB `message` are dropped; the collision path logs `archivedClientCollision: true` instead of the archived client's UUID (which would re-introduce the archive-enumeration linkage the visitor-facing message deliberately hides).

**Reused, not built:** `hashFingerprint` (`lib/portal/tokens.ts`) — the SAME salted SHA-256 helper + `PORTAL_FINGERPRINT_SALT` the portal magic-link logs already use (`app/portal/login/actions.ts` logs `emailHash: hashFingerprint(emailNormalized)`). It trims its input, returns `null` for empty input, and is **fail-closed in production** (returns `null` if the salt is unset — never a constant — with a one-time `portal_fingerprint_salt_missing` warning; stable dev fallback off-prod). Because the salt is shared, the same email yields the same fingerprint across portal + booking logs, so an operator can still correlate repeated failures for one booker **by value** without any raw email ever being written. This is **log minimization** (deterministic, salted) — not full anonymization. **Why:** `/book/[slug]` is reachable with no login, so its logs are the casual-exposure surface most worth minimizing. **Alternative considered:** a new booking-namespaced `fingerprintBookingEmail` wrapper — rejected as needless duplication; the portal helper already encodes the salt + fail-closed convention and the cross-surface correlation is a feature, not a leak.

**Scope / not done (deferred to a follow-up PII-log pass):** the equivalent raw-DB-`message` and raw-error-object logs on the OTHER public token surfaces — `app/cancel/[token]/actions.ts` (notably L413 logs the whole `emailErr`, which can carry the owner email), `app/reschedule/[token]/actions.ts` (L941 logs an arbitrary thrown `error.message`), `app/manage/[token]/actions.ts`, `app/intake/[token]/actions.ts` + `page.tsx`, and the shared `lib/email/send-appointment.ts` / `lib/sms/send-appointment.ts` failure logs. Also untouched **deliberately** (it is a DB write, not a log; changing it is a behavior/data change): `app/book/[slug]/actions.ts` L796-802 persists `{ source, email, notes }` into the `appointment_audit` table from the public path — flagged for a separate audit-minimization decision. **Pinned by** tests/app/book/pii-log-minimization.test.ts (source-pins: the two named payloads drop `normalizedEmail`/`archivedClientId`/raw `message` and carry `emailFingerprint` + `archivedClientCollision: true`; every `logInternalBookingError` payload is free of raw email / client id / DB message; `hashFingerprint` is imported from `@/lib/portal/tokens`) plus a determinism check (same email → same 64-hex fingerprint, never equal to the raw email). No Stripe/payments/billing/booking-business-rule change. Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Appointment cancel/reschedule token hashing at rest (PR #260, migration 0090)

**Decision (2026-06-15):** store appointment cancel/reschedule/manage tokens **hashed at rest**, closing a gap flagged in review (the high-entropy `appointments.cancellation_token` was stored raw and looked up directly, while portal-session and calendar-feed tokens are already hashed — migrations 0052 / 0079). This mirrors the **calendar-feed hash-at-rest playbook (migration 0079)** applied to appointments: new column `appointments.cancellation_token_hash` (SHA-256 hex, CHECK `^[a-f0-9]{64}$` or null, partial unique), `extensions.digest` backfill of every existing row, and a `BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE` trigger that auto-hashes a raw token whenever the hash is absent. The new TS helper `hashAppointmentToken` (lib/booking/appointment-token.ts) is the lookup hash; the migration's `extensions.digest` is the storage hash, and a DB-integration test pins that the two agree. **New appointments store ONLY the hash** (raw column NULL); public lookups hash the incoming URL token and match `cancellation_token_hash`. The raw `cancellation_token` column is **kept transitionally** — already-emitted `/cancel/<raw>` links in client inboxes still resolve (their hash was backfilled). **(Update: the raw column, the deploy-window trigger, and the dual-match raw RPC branches were removed in PR #264 / migration 0091 once the window closed; links still resolve via the backfilled hash.)**

**Compatibility / deploy window:** migration-first (apply 0090 in prod, verify column + backfill, then merge app). During the in-flight window an OLD app instance may still pass a raw token, so both token-verifying RPCs (`public_cancel_appointment_with_token`, `reschedule_appointment`) **dual-match `cancellation_token_hash = $token OR cancellation_token = $token`** (the branches are mutually exclusive — a 32-char base64url raw token can never equal a 64-hex hash), and the reschedule RPC's new-token INSERT routes by shape (64-hex → hash column; raw → raw column + trigger hashes it). The app resolvers are purely hash-based (the trigger + backfill guarantee every row is hash-lookupable).

**Render-time links (consequence of "raw only at creation"):** surfaces that rebuild a link AFTER creation can no longer read a raw token — the portal Manage button, reminder emails/SMS (cron), and internal-booking confirmations now mint the existing stateless **HMAC token** (`generateCancellationToken`, expires at the appointment start). To make those links work end to end, **`/reschedule` now accepts the HMAC fallback** that `/cancel` and `/manage` already had, removing the prior column-only asymmetry. The booking + reschedule CONFIRMATION emails still carry the raw token (generated in-memory at creation).

**Why:** a DB compromise must not yield usable cancel/reschedule bearer links. **Alternative considered:** hashing inside the RPC via pgcrypto under the hardened `search_path` — rejected as fragile (extension-schema resolution); the app computes the hash in TS (well-tested SHA-256) and passes it, keeping pgcrypto only in the backfill/trigger where 0079 already proved it safe. **Not done at the time (later resolved):** nulling/dropping the raw column — **done in PR #264 (migration 0091).** Still deferred: rotating live tokens, any UI. **No Stripe/payments/billing/booking-business-rule change; live payments remain disabled.**

### Imported treatment memory in Before Today (PR #259, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-15):** surface imported (paper/Jane/Fresha/spreadsheet) treatment memory inside the **Before Today** pre-treatment briefing on the client page, so the history Quick Import (PR #257) stores becomes useful before the next appointment. This delivers the "Before-Today surfacing" that PR #252 and PR #257 explicitly deferred. **Read-only surfacing only — no migration, no dependency, no schema/parser/importer change.** Reuses the PR #252 RLS-backed read helper `getImportedTreatmentMemoriesForClient` (studio+client-scoped, voided excluded by default, newest-first occurred_on desc nulls last → imported_at desc, honest pre-cap `totalFound`) and the `ImportedMemoryView` shape (safe provenance labels + `IMPORTED_PROVENANCE_NOTE` "Imported history, not charted live in Hone."). **Seam (deliberate):** the pure, sync, I/O-free `buildBeforeToday` assembler is left UNCHANGED (it is reused per-client in the dashboard preview loop, whose batched "exactly 3 reads" pin must not break); instead the only render site — `app/(app)/clients/[id]/page.tsx` (client Overview) — loads imported memory in its existing `Promise.all` (capped at 5) and passes it as a second prop to `BeforeTodayCard`, which renders a visually distinct **amber** "Imported treatment memory" section (NOT the blue Hone-charted styling) **after** the live-history block, so it shows even when there is no live charted history. Each row shows safe useful fields when present (source label, date, treatment area, modality, method/machine, probe type/size/lot, tolerance, reaction, caution, next-visit note, aftercare, imported note) with empties skipped, plus a "Showing the latest N of M imported records" line when capped and a "Review against the original record if needed." footer. **Not in scope (V1 read-only):** no edit/void/merge/attach-to-existing/convert-to-session, no batch-void UI, no dashboard-preview change (full card only, so the briefing stays scannable), no AI/OCR. **Privacy:** studio+client-scoped RLS reads only; voided rows excluded; no audit JSON, raw CSV/TSV, import internals, exposure incidents, payment internals, Stripe ids, or tokens; no service role. **Pinned by** tests/app/clients/before-today.test.ts (new imported-section source-pins: prop, labels + provenance note, separate-from-live rendering, safe-field display, cap copy, safe wording, and the page loading it RLS-scoped + capped + voided-excluded + passed to the card), the already-green tests/lib/imported-treatment-memory.test.ts (void/order/cap/labels — reused) + tests/db/imported-treatment-memory.db.test.ts (RLS + cross-studio isolation), and e2e/before-today-imported.spec.ts (import a client+history via Quick Import → open the client → the imported section shows, labelled not-charted-live, no overflow). Stripe gates unchanged (one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled; appointment reminders still depend on the external 15-minute scheduler (PR #258).

### Cron and reminder reliability fix (PR #258, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-15):** move the cron schedule in-repo to `vercel.json` and fix the 2h-reminder schedule/window mismatch. **Reliability hardening only — no migration, no dependency, no rewrite of the reminder system.** Two real defects: (1) `vercel.json` had `"crons": []` while three cron routes were active, so production reminders/breaks depended on an **external, unauditable scheduler** (`cron-job.org`, documented as living "outside the repo"); (2) the 2h reminder window was `now+105min … now+135min` (30 min wide) — under the assumed hourly cadence a 30-min window sampled every 60 min silently misses ~half of all appointment minute offsets. **Fix (+ a plan-limit finding during review):** the preview deploy revealed the **production Vercel plan caps cron cadence at once-per-day**, so a `*/15` reminder cron is rejected at deploy (Vercel points the failed deploy at its cron usage/pricing doc). A 2h-before reminder needs sub-daily checks, so — per the operator's decision — `vercel.json` schedules only the **daily** `/api/cron/materialize-recurring-breaks` (`0 8 * * *`, allowed on every plan), and `/api/cron/appointment-reminders` runs from an **external every-15-minute scheduler** (cron-job.org, `GET` with `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET`); if the project moves to Vercel Pro it becomes a `*/15` `vercel.json` cron (the `APPOINTMENT_REMINDER_CRON_SCHEDULE` constant). `/api/cron/no-show-check` is **intentionally left unscheduled** (disabled, non-mutating stub — scheduling a no-op would waste invocations and mislead). Vercel Cron auto-attaches `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}`, the external scheduler is configured to send it, and the existing `lib/cron/auth.ts` validates either — no auth change. The cadence + windows are now a single source of truth in `lib/cron/reminder-schedule.ts` (`APPOINTMENT_REMINDER_CRON_SCHEDULE`, `REMINDER_WINDOW_MINUTES`, `reminderWindowIso`, and the pure `uncoveredOffsets`/`windowCoversAllOffsets` invariant helpers), imported by both the route and the tests so the schedule and window cannot drift. The 2h window stays `[105,135]` (30 min) — at `*/15` that is `2× the cadence`, so every appointment minute offset is eligible at least once and a single skipped cron fire still leaves a grid point in-window (24h window `[23h,25h]` is safe at any cadence). **Claim-before-send unchanged** (`claimEmailSend` → send → `recordEmailResult`; 3-strike `MAX_ATTEMPTS`; stale-claim reclaim); idempotency is still owned by the claim. **Added:** (a) a `reminder_send_exhausted` ops alert (warning, via the existing `recordOpsAlert`) when a reminder hits `MAX_ATTEMPTS` without sending, carrying non-sensitive metadata only (studio_id, appointment_id, reminder_type, attempt_count, retryable, reason — no client email/phone/notes/token/free-text); (b) a cheap appointment `status='confirmed'` re-check immediately before send so a reminder is never sent for an appointment cancelled after the window query (skips without sending; the bumped claim attempt is harmless since a non-confirmed row is filtered out next run). **Alternative considered:** widening the 2h window instead of tightening cadence — rejected; `*/15` keeps the "~2h" timing honest while guaranteeing coverage. **Pinned by** tests/lib/cron/reminder-schedule.test.ts (every offset covered at `*/15`; hourly+30min fails; 24h safe; window = 2× cadence) and tests/app/cron-config.test.ts (vercel.json schedules each active route, no-show-check intentionally absent, the configured cadence covers the 2h window, claim-before-send + status re-check + exhausted-alert + cron auth pins). docs/08 + docs/10 updated to reflect in-repo Vercel cron. Stripe gates unchanged (one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled.

### Quick Import V1 (PR #257, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-14):** ship Quick Import V1 — an owner-only `/settings/import` that brings clients + basic historical treatment memory over from CSV/TSV **paste or file**, **preview-first**, into the existing PR #252 `imported_treatment_memories` schema. Migration is Hone's biggest adoption barrier; the V1 promise is "start using Hone without retyping your whole practice." **NOT** OCR, AI extraction, Jane/Fresha API sync, destructive merge, overwrite, live charting, or public self-serve onboarding. **Ponytail pass: no migration, no dependency, no service role.** Reuses the 0089 owner-INSERT RLS (owner writes `import_batches` + `imported_treatment_memories` via the normal authenticated client; the SECURITY DEFINER audit trigger auto-records events), the 0087 clients member-INSERT policy + `createClientAction`'s insert shape + per-studio email-dedup, the `assertOwner` role check, the settings owner-tab nav, and the `{ok,error}` client-island action pattern. **Pure pipeline** `lib/import/quick-import.ts` (a small local RFC4180-ish CSV/TSV parser — no CSV library; header aliases; loose date/phone/email/boolean normalization; identity grouping; existing-client dup detection; insert-shape mapping) is heavily unit-tested. **Two server actions** (`previewImportAction` returns a plan with NO writes; `confirmImportAction` re-derives from the same text and writes) run owner-gated through the RLS client. **Safety invariants:** create-only (confident duplicates — per-studio email, phone, or name+DOB — are **skipped**; weak name-only matches **warn**, never merge); never overwrite/merge/delete (0087 forbids client hard-delete, so rollback is **batch soft-void**, never a delete); multi-area paper-card rows **group into one client** by identity key, and same-name rows with conflicting email/phone/DOB are **never** auto-merged; one `imported_treatment_memories` row per content-bearing source row (client-only rows create no memory); imported rows are **never** written to `sessions`/`session_blocks`/`appointments` and are labelled "Imported history, not charted live in Hone."; the raw pasted text is parsed transiently and **never stored or logged**; a 2000-row cap; no AI/OCR/upload/external-fetch/SMS/email/payments. Confirm is **near-atomic** via bulk inserts (batch → bulk clients `.select(id)` matched by identity → bulk memories); on memory-insert failure the batch is soft-voided and the partial state reported honestly. One mapping note: `general_notes` → client `notes` (not duplicated into `imported_note`). **Deferred:** merge/update existing clients, batch-void UI, Before-Today surfacing of imported memory. **Pinned by** tests/lib/import/quick-import.test.ts (28: parsing/aliases/normalization/grouping/dedup/mapping/cap/safe-wording), tests/app/settings/import/quick-import-action.test.ts (owner gate before any write; RLS not service-role; writes only the 3 tables, never clinical/booking/payment; no AI/OCR/fetch/upload; no raw-text logging; soft-void not delete), tests/db/quick-import.db.test.ts (owner import write path + audit trigger + 0 sessions/appointments; non-owner cannot import), and e2e/quick-import.spec.ts (owner paste→preview→confirm creates grouped clients + memory; anonymous→`/login`; no-studio→`/no-access`). Stripe gates unchanged (one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled.

### Admin studio detail privacy follow-up (PR #256, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-14):** close the carried-forward privacy gap from PR #255 — the pre-existing `/admin/studios/[id]` detail page rendered raw client **names** (and selected `clients.select("id, name, created_at")`) — before Quick Import increases client volume. The page now shows operational metadata + **aggregate counts + setup-health flags only**, matching the Admin Console's privacy posture. **Ponytail pass: pure read-narrowing — no migration, no dependency; the new queries are cheaper than before.** Reuses the PR #255 console idiom (count cards, `Flag` setup flags), `createAdminClient()`, and the `app/admin/layout.tsx` `isAdmin` gate (unchanged). **Changes (one file, `app/admin/studios/[id]/page.tsx`):** `loadStudioDetail` replaces the three raw row selects (the leaky `clients` name select, the `practitioners` `*` table, the `pending_invitations` table) with ONE explicit-column `studios` select carrying embedded aggregate counts — `practitioners(count)/clients(count)/services(count)/studio_availability_default(count)/appointments(count)/imported_treatment_memories(count)` (every embedded table has a `studio_id` FK) — plus one owner `pending_invitations(status)` query to derive the owner-invite status. The page renders: a metadata grid (booking slug → `/book/<slug>`, timezone, owner email, owner-invite status), a counts grid (practitioners/clients/services/appointments/imported-memory — with an "Aggregate counts only" disclaimer), setup-health flags (Owner active / Services configured / Availability configured / Booking slug set / Live payments disabled), and links to the Admin Console + New Studio Wizard. It selects/renders **NO** client names, contact details, treatment notes, imported-memory contents, exposure incidents, payment internals, Stripe ids, tokens, or audit JSON. **Why counts:** an operator verifying setup needs "is the owner in? are services/availability configured? how many clients?" — counts answer that without exposing any client. **Alternative considered:** keep the practitioner/invitation tables (staff metadata, not a client leak) — rejected in favor of the brief's count-oriented allowed list + the stricter, simpler posture. **Pinned by** tests/app/admin/admin-studio-detail.test.ts (no client/clinical/payment/token/audit row reads; embedded counts; setup flags; layout gate) and e2e/new-studio-wizard.spec.ts (operator opens a seeded studio's detail page → counts/setup render, the seeded client name does NOT appear, no horizontal overflow). Admin remains operator-only; invite-only intact; Stripe gates unchanged (one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled.

### Admin Console V1 — studio setup + operator overview (PR #255, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-14):** upgrade `/admin` from a bare data dump into a small, useful internal **operator console** focused on invite-only studio setup, so the PR #254 New Studio Wizard is discoverable (no remembered URLs) and the operator can answer "which studios exist / which invites are pending / what's left before go-live." **NOT** public self-serve onboarding, billing/revenue analytics, Stripe onboarding, a CRM, email automation, team management, Quick Import, or per-client clinical analytics. **Ponytail pass: pure UI/read upgrade — no migration, no dependency, no new tracking, no server action.** Reuses the existing `app/admin/layout.tsx` `isAdmin` gate (the console inherits it), `createAdminClient()`, the existing embedded `(count)` aggregate pattern, and the page's table components. **Changes (all in `app/admin/page.tsx` + a nav link in `layout.tsx`):** a "Live payments are disabled." banner; a **Studio setup** card with a **Create new studio** CTA → `/admin/studios/new`; four overview count cards (total studios, pending owner invites, accepted owner invites, studios needing an owner); an enhanced studios table (name → detail, slug → `/book/<slug>`, owner email, timezone, owner-invite status, and per-studio **setup-health flags** Owner/Services/Availability) ; and operator quick links. All from **two cheap reads** — `studios` with embedded `practitioners(count)/clients(count)/services(count)/studio_availability_default(count)` (all have `studio_id` FKs) and an owner `pending_invitations(studio_id,status)` query — plus the pre-existing practitioners/waitlist/demo sections, kept as-is. **Data-privacy posture:** the console shows ONLY operational metadata + aggregate counts (studio names, slugs, owner emails, timezone, invite status, setup flags). It selects NO client names/contacts, treatment notes, exposure incidents, imported-memory contents, payment internals, Stripe ids, tokens, or audit JSON (pinned by negative source greps). **Deferred:** charts/analytics, search/filter/pagination, "upcoming appointments" (needs per-row time filters — not a setup signal), and any per-client clinical reads. **Alternative considered:** a separate `/admin/studios` index route + a dashboard framework — rejected (fold the list into `/admin`; counts + links, not a system). **Pinned by** tests/app/admin/admin-console.test.ts (console surface, the CTA, the cheap counts, and the NO-client-data negatives) and e2e/new-studio-wizard.spec.ts (operator sees the console, no horizontal overflow, "Create new studio" → the wizard). The existing tests/app/admin/new-studio-wizard.test.ts (operator-only wizard) and tests/app/admin/ops-alerts-page.test.ts (layout gate) remain green. Stripe gates unchanged (one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled.

### Internal New Studio Wizard V1 (PR #254, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-14):** automate ONLY the operator's manual studio bootstrap (docs/20 §2.1/§2.2) behind an internal, operator-only `/admin/studios/new` page — NOT public self-serve onboarding, billing, plan selection, Stripe Connect, team management, or invite-email automation. **Ponytail pass: almost entirely reuse, zero new dependency, no migration.** Reuses the existing operator gate `isAdmin`/`ADMIN_EMAILS` (`lib/admin.ts`, fail-closed in production — explicitly NOT a new `HONE_OPERATOR_EMAILS`), the `app/admin/layout.tsx` gate (so the wizard inherits it), the `assertAdmin` re-check idiom, and `createAdminClient()` (`lib/supabase/admin-server.ts`, `server-only`). **No migration:** the wizard inserts into the existing `studios` + `pending_invitations` tables via the service-role client (studios has no INSERT policy and pending_invitations INSERT is owner-only, so RLS blocks any normal user — confirmed across all migrations; the service-role path is the same one the manual runbook uses). **Why service-role not an RPC:** the operator gate is an env allowlist a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC can't read, so an app-level `isAdmin` gate + service-role is the stronger, simpler boundary; an RPC would add a migration for no benefit. **Server flow:** one `use server` action `createStudioWithOwnerInvite` — (1) `getUser()` + `isAdmin` (throws if not), (2) `parseNewStudioInput` (pure, unit-tested: required fields, lowercase/url-safe/non-reserved slug, valid IANA timezone via `Intl`, normalized email), (3) slug-uniqueness + duplicate-pending-invite pre-checks, (4) service-role insert `studios` (name, owner_email, slug, timezone only — fees NULL, no Stripe), (5) service-role insert `pending_invitations` role `owner`; on invitation failure it compensating-deletes the just-created (empty, owner-less) studio so no orphan is left. The owner practitioner row is **never** inserted directly — the existing `handle_new_user()` trigger creates it (with terms/privacy stamps) on the owner's invited first sign-in, unchanged. **One PR #253 touch (flagged):** the PR #253 middleware no-studio gate bounced any authenticated user without a practitioner row to `/no-access`, including a studio-less operator, so `/admin` was unreachable for them. Added a narrow, fail-closed carve-out: `isAdmin` operators may reach `/admin/*` without a studio. This does NOT weaken invite-only for anyone else — the carve-out is limited to `/admin` paths AND an admin email, the `/admin` layout + every admin action re-check `isAdmin`, and a no-studio NON-admin still goes to `/no-access`; it also fixes a latent post-#253 admin lockout. **Alternative considered:** a public/self-serve studio-creation flow — rejected (Hone stays invite-only for the supervised pilot). **Pinned by** tests/lib/studios/new-studio.test.ts (validation), tests/app/admin/new-studio-wizard.test.ts (operator gate before any write, the two writes, never inserts practitioners, no payment columns, the middleware carve-out, no public route), tests/db/new-studio-wizard.db.test.ts (normal user cannot insert studio/invitation; service-role path creates them and handle_new_user provisions the owner with safe defaults), and e2e/new-studio-wizard.spec.ts (anonymous → `/login`, no-studio non-operator → `/no-access`, operator creates a studio, reserved slug rejected). Stripe gates unchanged (one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled; no paid onboarding, no email automation.

### Invite-only auth and no-studio gate (PR #253, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-14):** Hone is NOT ready for public self-serve signup or public studio creation — Chloe (Willow) and Laura are the supervised pilot, and new studios are set up manually via the service-role path (and the future internal New Studio Wizard), never via signup. This PR audits and hardens the posture so Hone is clearly invite-only, BEFORE the New Studio Wizard. **Audit finding — the DB layer was already invite-only hardened** (so NO migration was needed): `handle_new_user()` (migration 0081 / PR #189) creates a practitioner ONLY when a matching `pending_invitations` row exists — an uninvited sign-in (e.g. Google OAuth, which cannot pass `shouldCreateUser`) yields an `auth.users` row but NO studio/practitioner; the magic-link path gates `shouldCreateUser` on a pending invitation (uninvited emails get the same generic "sent" state, no enumeration); RLS leaves `studios` with **no INSERT policy** (no authenticated user can create a studio), and `practitioners` / `pending_invitations` allow INSERT only for `is_studio_owner` (a no-studio or non-owner user cannot self-add, invite, or escalate). There is no `/signup` or `/register` route and no "Create account / Create studio / Start free" CTA anywhere. **The real gaps were app-level UX/guarding:** (1) a no-studio authenticated user hit a raw 500 (`getCurrentPractitionerWithStudio()` THREW "No active practitioner") instead of a friendly gate. **Changes (no business-rule/schema/payment touch):** (a) added a redirecting guard `requirePractitionerWithStudio()` (anon → `/login`, authed-no-studio → `/no-access`) used by the app-shell layout; `getCurrentPractitionerWithStudio()` keeps THROWING as the backstop for direct server-action POSTs (those run inside try/catch and return a generic denial — changing it to redirect risked a swallowed `NEXT_REDIRECT` and log noise). (b) Added the studio gate to **middleware** so a no-studio user is redirected to `/no-access` BEFORE any (app) layout/page renders (race-free; no page throws; no studio data ever loaded); exempts public routes, `/no-access`, and its sign-out POST; the shell-layout guard stays as defense in depth. (c) New `app/(auth)/no-access/page.tsx` — a safe "No studio access yet" gate (invite-only copy, Sign out, Contact Hone; does its OWN lightweight check — anon → `/login`, has-studio → `/dashboard` — never calls the shell guard, so no loop; renders no app nav, reads no studio data, `noindex`). (d) The login page gained explicit invite-only copy ("Invited users only. Use the email address your studio invitation was sent to." / "Need access, or trouble signing in? Email hello@hone.care"); auth methods unchanged (Google OAuth + gated magic link; no password/`signUp`). **Public booking and the marketing site are unchanged** (still public for configured studios). **Pinned by** e2e/invite-only.spec.ts (login invite-only copy + no signup CTA; an uninvited real sign-in lands on `/no-access`, every app route redirects there, no app nav leaks, sign-out → `/login`, anonymous → `/login`), tests/db/invite-only-posture.db.test.ts (9: no-studio/member cannot create studio/practitioner/invitation or escalate; owner CAN invite; handle_new_user provisions from an invitation — the invited first sign-in), and tests/app/invite-only-auth.test.ts (source pins on login copy, the guards, the middleware gate, and the /no-access page). The Stripe gates are unchanged (one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled; no paid self-serve onboarding.

### Imported Treatment Memory schema (PR #252, migration 0089)

**Decision (2026-06-14):** Hone's biggest adoption barrier is migration — practitioners already hold years of history in paper cards, Jane, Fresha, Square, GlossGenius, Mangomint, or spreadsheets. Before building a Quick Import flow (PR #253), Hone needs a SAFE DESTINATION for imported historical treatment memory, deliberately separate from live charting. **Why not reuse `sessions` / `session_blocks`:** `sessions.practitioner_id` is NOT NULL and `modality` is CHECK-constrained to `('electrolysis','laser')`, `started_at` defaults to `now()`, and sessions drive forward-looking/clinical surfaces (next_session_note, aftercare stamps) and per-block charting — none of which fit flat, messy, possibly-incomplete, manually-typed paper/Jane rows. Writing imports as live charted records would corrupt the moat (treatment memory charted IN Hone). **This PR is schema + read-model only** — no importer UI, no CSV/TSV parser, no OCR, no AI extraction, no image upload, no third-party API sync, no scraping, no client merge, no Before Today / Treatment Intelligence surfacing (all PR #253+). **Migration 0089 adds three tables:** (1) `import_batches` — REQUIRED because the batch is the unit of void/rollback when a paste goes wrong (duplicate export, wrong column mapping, wrong studio's file, incomplete rows, a test file imported by accident); carries `source_type` (CHECK in paper_card/jane/fresha/spreadsheet/other), provenance, `row_count`, `created_by`, `completed_at`, and soft-void columns (`voided_at`/`voided_by`/`void_reason`). (2) `imported_treatment_memories` — the durable rows; `client_id` is REQUIRED (the future importer must resolve/create the client first; conservative identity matching and duplicate/multi-area grouping remain a PR #253 risk) and `import_batch_id` is REQUIRED `on delete restrict`; clinical-ish values are kept as TEXT on purpose (`treatment_area_text`, `modality`, `method_or_machine`, `probe_type/size/lot`, `tolerance_text`, `reaction_text`, `caution_note`, `next_visit_note`, `imported_note`) plus a clean `occurred_on date` AND a messy `occurred_on_text`, because imported paper/export data does not match Hone's structured charting format — explicit columns (not JSONB) for queryability, RLS clarity, testing, and future surfacing; per-row soft-void columns. (3) `imported_treatment_memory_audit_events` — a DEDICATED append-only audit trail mirroring the 0086 record-keeping pattern, kept SEPARATE so this migration touches nothing in the Record Keeping audit infra (the generic `record_keeping_audit_row()` references `created_by_practitioner_id`, which the import tables do not have); written ONLY by a SECURITY DEFINER trigger that reuses the read-only `record_keeping_audit_actor()` helper. **Stores NO raw CSV/TSV, file contents, paper scans, images, OCR output, or external payloads.** **RLS (studio-scoped, mirroring 0001 helpers):** SELECT = any active member (`is_studio_member`); INSERT + UPDATE = owner only (`is_studio_owner`) — importing/migrating and voiding/correcting are owner actions; NO delete policy on any table, so nobody (owner included) can hard-delete imported history — correction is soft voiding. The audit table is SELECT-only for members (append-only). **Read helper** `lib/imported-treatment-memory.ts` (pure builder + RLS-backed loaders) excludes voided rows by default, orders `occurred_on desc nulls last` then `imported_at desc`, caps results, and returns safe provenance labels ("Imported from paper card", "Imported history, not charted live in Hone.") with recorded-history wording only — no advice/verified/compliance wording, no service role, no audit JSON / payment / token / exposure exposure. **Generated types:** added `ImportBatch`, `ImportedTreatmentMemory`, `ImportedTreatmentMemoryAuditEvent` to `lib/types/database.ts` and registered all three in `scripts/check-db-types.mjs` `CURATED_TABLES`; the drift check enforces an exact column-name match (29/13/12 cols). **Pinned by** tests/migrations/0089-imported-treatment-memory.test.ts (source-grep shape: tables/columns/CHECK/required-FK/owner-only-RLS/no-delete/audit-trigger/no-raw-blob), tests/lib/imported-treatment-memory.test.ts (pure builder: void-exclusion, ordering, cap, provenance labels, safe wording, read-only/no-provider source pins), and tests/db/imported-treatment-memory.db.test.ts (18 live RLS proofs on the real migrated DB: owner-only insert/update, member read, cross-studio isolation, no hard delete + rows survive, audit 'created'/'updated' with actor, append-only + member-readable + cross-studio-isolated audit, source_type CHECK, required client_id, on-delete-restrict batch FK). **Migration-first:** apply 0089 to production and verify tables/policies/grants/types posture BEFORE merging app code (this PR's app code is a read helper only, not yet referenced by any surface). Quick Import V1 (PR #253) will need a conservative client-matching strategy and must avoid false merges. The Stripe gates are unchanged (exactly one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled.

### Origin-story section visual fix (PR #250 follow-up, public-only)

**Decision (2026-06-14):** PR #250's "Built from real treatment rooms." homepage section shipped as a text-only, left-weighted block that left a large empty band to the right and below — it read as unfinished/half-loaded. Rebuilt as a balanced two-column product story matching the rest of the page's rhythm: left column keeps the eyebrow ("Why Hone exists") + heading + tightened body ("…Hone keeps that memory close to the next visit.") + a short supporting line ("The calendar moves on. The details should not."); right column adds a compact **"What gets hard to remember"** card (generic question rows — What was used last time? / How did the client tolerate it? / What needs caution next time? / Was the record complete? — then a blue memory band "Hone keeps those details close to the next appointment." and chips Used last time · Client response · Caution note · Record gap). Uses the existing MockCard / Chip / RememberBand visual language; generic labels only, no client names, no fake metrics/proof, no salon/practice-management broadening, no AI/agentic tone. Stacks copy-then-card on mobile, no horizontal overflow at 390px or 1280px. **Visual/layout only:** no change to the Pilot Love Loop feedback behavior, the mailto builder, the Dashboard, or any app/payment/RLS/schema posture. Pins updated in tests/app/marketing-homepage.test.ts (visual card present) and e2e/marketing-homepage.spec.ts (section + card visible). Shipped as a follow-up PR on top of merged PR #250. Live payments remain disabled.

### Pilot Love Loop V1 (PR #250, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-14):** with the core treatment-memory loop and two safe agentic-style workflows shipped (Daily Prep Brief PR #241, Follow-up Assistant PR #249), the next priority is **practitioner love before growth** — learning whether the supervised pilot (Chloe, Laura) is reaching the "I'd hate to lose this" moment. This adds a tiny, MANUAL feedback layer around Hone's highest-value treatment-memory surfaces. It is explicitly **NOT** a referral engine, growth automation, AI, or marketing automation. **No migration, no persistence, no analytics provider, no automated send, no contact access, no referral/tracking links.** Everything is a plain `mailto:hello@hone.care` link (the established in-app support address) that opens the practitioner's OWN mail client. **Architecture:** a pure builder `buildPilotFeedbackMailto(surface, intent)` (`lib/pilot/feedback-mailto.ts`) that takes ENUM inputs only (surface ∈ before_today/daily_prep/follow_up_assistant/dashboard_pilot_learning; intent ∈ useful/not_useful/general/another_electrologist), so by construction NO client name, contact detail, treatment note, exposure detail, payment internal, Stripe id, raw token, or audit JSON can ever reach the subject/body; the body carries only the surface label + sentiment + an empty "(Add any details here.)" placeholder for the practitioner's own words. **Surfaces (deliberately only two + a card, not everywhere):** a quiet server-component `PilotFeedbackPrompt` ("Was this useful? · Yes · Not really · Tell us what it helped with") on the **Daily Prep Brief** and **Follow-up Assistant** dashboard cards, and a compact **"Pilot learning"** dashboard card ("Notice a moment where Hone helped you remember something? Send it to Sam." with "Send feedback" + "Know another electrologist?" — both manual mailto, no contact import/referral link) near the bottom of the Dashboard (well below Today). **Before Today was deliberately deferred** to keep the clinical client page uncluttered. Both components are pure server components (plain mailto anchors — no "use client", no onClick, no modal/popup, no external script, no analytics). **No role gating built** (the spec said don't build complex permissions); shown to all authenticated dashboard users. **Marketing (optional, §5):** a short, human origin-story section on the public homepage — "Built from real treatment rooms." / "Hone started with a simple problem: after enough appointments, the important details get hard to keep in your head…" — copy only, no claims/metrics/invented proof, preserving the treatment-memory positioning and not broadening into generic salon/practice-management software (8th `SectionShell`). **Safety:** nothing is sent/charged/mutated; reads nothing sensitive; no AI/model/provider; no booking/intake/charting/Calendar/Record-Keeping/payment/RLS/schema change. Pinned by tests/lib/pilot/feedback-mailto.test.ts (9: enum-only safety, no sensitive data in any mailto, link-only components, no automated-send/contact/referral, dashboard wiring) and tests/app/marketing-homepage.test.ts (origin-story section), and exercised by the core memory loop E2E (Pilot learning card + safe Send-feedback mailto + "Was this useful?" prompt render on the dashboard). Stripe gates unchanged (one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled.

### Missing Records / Follow-up Assistant V1 (PR #249, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-14):** the SECOND agentic-style workflow from the docs/22 roadmap (after Daily Prep Brief, PR #241), and deliberately the same posture: RULES-BASED ONLY — no AI, no model call, no provider integration (Anthropic/OpenAI/Gemini), no chatbot, no autonomous action, no writes, no sends, no charges. It answers, from already-recorded Hone data, "what recorded workflow gaps and follow-ups remain around recent appointments?" so the practitioner can finish the record. **Five V1 item types, priority-ordered:** 1 completed appointment with no charted session ("Charting needed"), 2 recorded session with aftercare/risks not marked ("Aftercare not marked"), 3 recorded treatment area with no probe lot ("Probe lot missing"), 4 intake started but not submitted ("Intake incomplete", `in_progress` only — `submitted` is "awaiting review", already covered by the existing Needs-attention card), 5 for-next-visit note with no upcoming appointment ("Follow-up"). **The caution/watch-note item type was deliberately DEFERRED:** "Clients needing attention" (PR #214) already covers it, and the spec says skip what duplicates. **Architecture (mirrors PR #214 / #241):** a pure deterministic builder `buildMissingRecordsAssistant` (rules + priority order + dedup to one item per client/type keeping the most recent + cap 6 + safe wording; no I/O, no model, no mutation) and a bounded loader `getMissingRecordsAssistant` in `lib/dashboard/missing-records-assistant.ts`. The gap rules MIRROR the existing ones so the surfaces never disagree: probe-lot / aftercare from `lib/sessions/before-today.ts`; completed-not-charted from `lib/dashboard/next-action.ts`. **Loader reads (read-only, studio-scoped, RLS-backed, no new query is heavy):** recent sessions (cap 120, newest-first) + their `session_blocks` (probe lot + treatment-area presence); recent completed appointments (30-day window, cap 100) with a `session_blocks!inner` join to tell charted from uncharted; upcoming appointments for clients with a for-next-visit note; latest intake status per scanned client. Window computed once at the call site (`new Date().toISOString()`) so the helper stays clock-free. **UI:** a compact "Follow-up assistant" card (`app/(app)/dashboard/follow-up-assistant.tsx`) under Practice Snapshot (Today stays on top; named "Follow-up assistant", NOT "Needs attention", to avoid colliding with the existing intake/services/Stripe card), each item a chip + short recorded-history reason + one link to an existing safe route (`/clients/<id>`, `/clients/<id>/sessions/<sid>`, `/clients/<id>/sessions/new?appointment_id=<aid>`); "Showing N of M recorded gaps" when capped; calm empty state "Nothing needs follow-up right now." **Safety (docs/22):** assistant not decider, flag not diagnose, summarize recorded history (do not invent); recorded-history wording only (recorded / not marked / missing / for next visit / charting needed); reads no sensitive surface (no exposure incidents, no payment internals, no Stripe ids, no raw tokens, no audit JSON); studio-scoped via the same RLS as the rest of the Dashboard; no service-role, no public route, no model/provider call; writes nothing and sends nothing. Pinned by tests/lib/dashboard/missing-records-assistant.test.ts (21: item types, priority/dedup/cap, safe routes + wording, no-write / no-provider / no-sensitive-surface / studio-scoped source pins, dashboard wiring) and exercised by the core memory loop E2E (the Follow-up assistant section renders on the dashboard, running the real bounded loader). The Stripe gates are unchanged (exactly one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled.

Micro-context labels ("Appointment data" vs "Treatment memory") make the contrast immediately readable. The now-unused `CheckRow`/`DashRow` atoms were removed. Hone helps you remember what matters." / "Built with electrologists who do this every day."), calendar-vs-Hone ("Your calendar shows the appointment. Hone shows what to remember." + the "Most tools stop at the appointment" paragraph), before/during/after ("Before, during, and after the appointment." with one plain sentence per card), what Hone remembers ("What Hone remembers." with the Daily Prep card reworded "Daily Prep Brief is simple on purpose."), records ("Pull the record when you need it." / "Choose the client, review the record, and print it." keeping the local public-health caveat and lot traceability), the former Agentic section reframed as "Smarter prep, without autopilot." ### Daily Prep Brief V1 (PR #241, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-12):** the first agentic-style workflow from the docs/22 roadmap, and deliberately RULES-BASED ONLY: no AI, no model call, no provider integration (Anthropic/OpenAI/Gemini), no chatbot, no autonomous action. A daily preparation brief that answers, from already-recorded Hone data, who is on today, who is returning, what to remember before each appointment, which appointments need intake or charting attention, and which records are missing. **Smallest useful V1, zero new queries:** a pure helper (`lib/dashboard/daily-prep-brief.ts`, `buildDailyPrepBrief`) turns facts the Dashboard already loads (visible appointments, the Before Today preview pipeline, the linked-session charting state from PR #236, and intake status) into a deterministic, priority-ordered list. To avoid a new query, the Before Today preview (`lib/dashboard/before-today-previews.ts`) gained three passthrough fields already computed by `buildBeforeToday` (nextVisitNote, cautionNote, granular reminders); the compact Today-row preview is unchanged. **Priority order:** 1 upcoming with a next-visit or caution note, 2 upcoming with intake not reviewed, 3 completed needing charting, 4 a missing-record reminder, 5 new client with no prior memory, 6 already charted. **UI:** a compact "Daily prep brief" card rendered directly under Today (so Today stays first), each item linking only to the existing `/clients/<id>` route; it never duplicates the full Before Today card. **Empty state:** "Nothing needs special review yet." Calm, never an error. **Safety (docs/22):** assistant not decider, flag not diagnose, summarize recorded history (do not invent); recorded-history wording only (For next visit, Caution noted, Last recorded, Intake incomplete, Charting needed, Probe lot missing, Aftercare not marked, No prior treatment history yet); reads no sensitive surface (no exposure incidents, no payment internals, no Stripe ids, no raw tokens, no audit JSON); studio-scoped via the same RLS as the rest of the Dashboard; no service-role, no public route, no model/provider call; writes nothing and sends nothing. Pinned by tests/lib/dashboard/daily-prep-brief.test.ts (helper + wiring + safety) and exercised by both E2E specs (the brief surfaces the saved next-visit memory; the card fits the phone and links to the client). The Stripe gates are unchanged (exactly one paymentIntents.create). Live payments remain disabled.

### Agentic readiness and safety plan (PR #240, docs-only)

**Decision (2026-06-12):** with the post-hardening and mobile pilot polish sequence complete through PR #239, the next strategic direction is to make Hone agentic, but safely. The goal is an operating memory system for electrologists that can prepare, flag, summarize, and draft from recorded treatment history, NOT a generic chatbot and NOT a system that decides anything clinical, sends anything, or moves money. **This PR is docs/design only: no AI runtime, no model call, no endpoint, no migration, no schema, no RLS change, no payment capability is added.** It adds `docs/22_AGENTIC_READINESS_AND_SAFETY.md`, which fixes the rules any future agentic build must satisfy before a line of agentic code is written: the product principle (assistant not decider; draft not send; flag not diagnose; summarize recorded history, do not invent; prepare the practitioner, do not prescribe; require human confirmation before any external action; never silently mutate clinical history; never auto-charge; never auto-message clients); the safe read surfaces (today's appointments, client basics, Before Today, Treatment Intelligence, session blocks, treatment-area history, probe/lot, tolerance/reaction/caution, next-session notes, missing-record reminders, intake status, already-visible record gaps); the excluded sensitive surfaces (exposure incident details and audit payloads, payment internals, Stripe ids, raw appointment tokens, raw calendar feed tokens, auth/session data, unscoped audit JSON, anything cross-studio or outside RLS), mirroring the Global Search V1 exclusions (PR #232) and the exposure-incident owner tier (PR #222, migration 0088); the hard prohibitions (no treatment-setting recommendations as medical advice, no safe/unsafe claims, no diagnosis, no causation, no silent clinical mutation, no deletes, no auto-send, no auto-charge, no unconfirmed appointment create/change, no exposure detail exposure to non-owners); per-action human confirmation rules; the safe-wording vocabulary; the first three future workflows (Daily Prep Brief V1, Missing Records / Follow-up Assistant, Draft-only Client Message Assistant); and the audit + RLS posture (same studio-scoped access, no service-role broad search, no cross-studio memory, no public AI endpoints, exposure incidents stay owner-tiered, payments stay off). **The agentic roadmap therefore starts with a safety plan, not a runtime.** The next possible PR after this is Daily Prep Brief V1, built read-and-draft only under every rule in docs/22. Docs-only PR; pinned by tests/docs/agentic-readiness.test.ts. Live payments remain disabled.

### Chloe pilot feedback cleanup (PR #238, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-12):** Chloe's live mobile retest was strongly positive (she would use Hone "in a heartbeat now" and knows "nothing like it on the market"); five concrete friction items remained, all fixed UX-only. **1) iOS search zoom:** the mobile search input was text-sm (14px); iOS Safari auto-zooms focused inputs under 16px and stays zoomed. The input is now text-base; user zoom stays fully enabled (no viewport meta tricks). **2) Client sections on phones:** the PR #233 one-row tab scroller still moved under the finger; phones now get a native Section select (all six sections, 16px, 44px tall, same pick() URL navigation) while md+ keeps the underlined tab row. **3) End of charting:** the session page saves per piece (each treatment area, the next-visit note, the aftercare stamp), so there was nothing to submit at the end and Chloe hunted for a save. A Finish up section above Delete now says that plainly and offers exits: Done charting (client Sessions tab) and, when the session has appointment context, Review appointment & billing (the calendar appointment with the PR #181 NextStepCard). Links only: no new write path, no sticky bar, no double-submit. **4) Records copy:** section label/heading is now "Procedure records" with helper "Use this when you need a procedure record for one client", the picker label is "Choose a client", and the filtered hint says "print this client's procedure record" when a client is chosen; filter, caps, and the formal printed title ("Client Records for Invasive Procedures") are unchanged. **5) Dashboard hierarchy:** Today moved to the top (it sat under snapshot/attention/booking/birthday cards); the Getting started card renders below Today only while auto-detected steps remain, and collapses to a one-line "Setup complete" footer link once autoDone === autoTotal (the /getting-started route and its full checklist are untouched and stay in the menus). Practice Snapshot, Charted within 24h, and the PR #236 next actions are unchanged. Both E2E specs cover the new behavior (computed 16px font checks, selectOption navigation, Finish up in both link branches, records copy, Today-above-snapshot geometry). Live payments remain disabled.

### Before Today visual hierarchy (PR #237, no migration)

**Decision (2026-06-12):** the Before Today card (PR #211) put "Last treated" first, buried the latest tolerance/reaction as small text inside the Remember today band, joined the setup into one long line without probe lot or minutes, and never showed reaction notes, even though all of that data was already loaded on both surfaces. **Reorder, not re-source:** the pure assembler (lib/sessions/before-today.ts) now emits the briefing in pre-treatment reading order and the card renders it that way: 1) Remember today (watch lines + For-next-visit note) first and visually dominant; 2) Last treatment snapshot (date, areas, and wrapping chips for modality, frequency, probe, lot, energy level, total recorded minutes); 3) Client response (last recorded) with tolerance/reaction chips and the last treatment's reaction notes; 4) Record reminders unchanged. Two pure input fields were added (blockMinutes, blockReactionNotes), both mapped from block columns the client page and the Dashboard preview pipeline already select; no new queries, no schema, no rule changes. The new-client empty state now says treatment memory will appear after the first charted session. **Dashboard Today preview deliberately unchanged:** its three lines (Remember / Latest setup / Records) already match the hierarchy and rows must stay short; latestSetupLine is kept on the briefing for it. Wording discipline pinned (recorded/last recorded/not recorded/for next visit; never recommended/caused/diagnosis). Both E2E specs assert the new sections render with charted data and the phone viewport never overflows. Live payments remain disabled.

### Dashboard Today next actions (PR #236, no migration)

Linked-session facts come from two batched studio-scoped reads (same shape as the charted-24h loader; no N+1). **Markup:** the row body keeps its calendar-detail link; the action is a sibling button (no nested anchors), wrapping below the content on phones. The Before Today preview, intake pill, and pinned-note line are unchanged (hierarchy already matched the spec). Safe wording pinned. **Browser proof of both worlds:** the mobile spec shows the action flipping from "Open client" to "Review Before Today" after client-path (unlinked) charting; the core spec shows "View session" + "Charted" for appointment-context charting. Live payments remain disabled.
